### [Ms-174](/ms-174/#39r.4) <span class="fac">[39r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39r.webp)</span> {#ms-174-39r-4}

It seems to me that whoever doubts the existence of the Earth at that time must attack the essence of all historical evidence. And I cannot say that this is certainly _true_.


### [Ms-174](/ms-174/#39r.5+39v.1) <span class="fac">[39r\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39r.webp),[39v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-174-39r-5--39v-1}

At some point, one must move from explanation to mere description.


### [Ms-174](/ms-174/#39v.2) <span class="fac">[39v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-174-39v-2}

What we call historical evidence suggests that the Earth existed long before my birth; – the opposite hypothesis has _nothing_ to support it.


### [Ms-174](/ms-174/#39v.3) <span class="fac">[39v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-174-39v-3}

If now everything speaks for a hypothesis, and nothing against it, – is it then certainly true? One can describe it that way. – But does it certainly correspond to reality, to the facts? – With this question, you are already moving in a circle.


### [Ms-174](/ms-174/#39v.4) <span class="fac">[39v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-174/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-174-39v-4}

There is, of course, justification; but the justification has an end.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#1r.1) <span class="fac">[1r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-1r-1}

What does it mean to say that the truth of a sentence is _certain_?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#1r.2+1r.3+1v.1) <span class="fac">[1r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1r.webp),[1r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1r.webp),[1v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-1r-2--1r-3--1v-1}

With the word “certain,” we express complete conviction, the absence of all doubt, & we use it to convince others. That is _subjective_ certainty. But when is something objectively certain? – When error is impossible. But what kind of possibility is that? Must not error be _logically_ excluded?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#1r.2+1r.3+1v.1.2) <span class="fac">[1r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1r.webp),[1r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1r.webp),[1v\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-1r-2--1r-3--1v-1-2}

If I believe I am sitting in my room & it is not the case, then one would not say that I was _mistaken_: But what is the essential difference between a mistake & this case?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#1v.3+2r.1) <span class="fac">[1v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/1v.webp),[2r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-1v-3--2r-1}

Certain evidence is that which we accept as absolutely certain, according to which we act with certainty without doubt. What we call “error” plays a very specific role in our language-games, & so does what we consider to be certain evidence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#2r.2) <span class="fac">[2r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-2r-2}

It would be wrong to say that we consider something as secure evidence because it is certainly true.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#2r.3+2v.1) <span class="fac">[2r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2r.webp),[2v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-2r-3--2v-1}

Rather, we must first determine the role of making a decision for or against a sentence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#2v.2) <span class="fac">[2v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-2v-2}

The use of “true or false” has something misleading about it, because it is as if one said “it agrees with the facts or not,” and yet the question is precisely what “agreement” is here.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#2v.3+3r.1) <span class="fac">[2v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/2v.webp),[3r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/3r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-2v-3--3r-1}

“The sentence is true or false” actually only means that a decision for or against it must be possible. But that does not say, on what grounds one decides for or against it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#3r.2) <span class="fac">[3r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/3r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-3r-2}

Imagine someone asking: “Is it really true that we rely on the evidence of our memory (or our senses) as we do?”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#3v.1) <span class="fac">[3v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/3v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-3v-1}

Moore’s certain sentences almost say that we have a right to rely on this evidence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#3v.2) <span class="fac">[3v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/3v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-3v-2}

What we call historical evidence suggests that the Earth existed long before my birth. The opposite hypothesis has _no_ support.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#3v.3+4r.1) <span class="fac">[3v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/3v.webp),[4r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/4r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-3v-3--4r-1}

A hypothesis for which everything speaks in favor, against which nothing speaks – is it then objectively true? One can _call_ it that. But does it _unconditionally_ agree with the facts? At best, it shows us what “agree” means. We find it difficult to imagine it as false, but also difficult to make an application of it. – – – With this question, you are already moving in a circle.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#4v.1) <span class="fac">[4v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/4v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-4v-1}

What is this agreement, if not that what is evidence in this language-game speaks in favor of our sentence? (Log. Phil. Abh.)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#4v.2+5r.1) <span class="fac">[4v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/4v.webp),[5r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-4v-2--5r-1}

But the justification of the evidence comes to an end; but the end is not that certain sentences immediately appear to us as true, that is, a kind of _seeing_ on our part, but our _action_, which lies at the basis of the language-game.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#5r.2) <span class="fac">[5r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-5r-2}

If the true is the justified, then the ground is not _true_, nor false.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#5r.3+5v.1) <span class="fac">[5r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5r.webp),[5v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-5r-3--5v-1}

If someone asked us “But is that _true_?”, we could say to him “Yes”; and if he demanded reasons, we could say “I cannot give you any reasons, but if you learn more, you will also be of this opinion.” If he did not come to that, it would mean that he, for example, cannot learn history.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#5v.2) <span class="fac">[5v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-5v-2}

“Strange coincidence that all the people whose heads one has looked into had a brain!”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#5v.3+6r.1) <span class="fac">[5v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/5v.webp),[6r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-5v-3--6r-1}

I have a telephone conversation with New York. My friend tells me that his little tree has the so-and-so buds. I am now convinced that the little tree is … Am I also convinced that the Earth exists?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#6r.2) <span class="fac">[6r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-6r-2}

The fact that the Earth exists is rather part of the whole _picture_, which is the _basis_ of my belief.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#6r.3) <span class="fac">[6r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-6r-3}

Does my telephone conversation with New York confirm my conviction that the Earth exists?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#6r.4+6v.1) <span class="fac">[6r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6r.webp),[6v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-6r-4--6v-1}

Some things seem to us to be established and they drop out of consideration. They are, as it were, put on a siding.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#6v.2+7r.1) <span class="fac">[6v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/6v.webp),[7r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-6v-2--7r-1}

It is now the form of our way of looking at things and characterizes all our questions and investigations. It may once have been disputed. Perhaps, however, it has belonged to the _framework_ of all our considerations for time immemorial. (Every human being has parents.)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#7r.2) <span class="fac">[7r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-7r-2}

For example, we consider a calculation to be sufficiently checked under certain circumstances. What gives us the right to do so? Experience? Could it not deceive us? We must end justifying somewhere, and then the sentence remains: that we _so_ calculate.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#7r.3+7v.1) <span class="fac">[7r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7r.webp),[7v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-7r-3--7v-1}

Our ‘experience sentences’ do not form a homogeneous mass.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#7v.2) <span class="fac">[7v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-7v-2}

What is wrong with the idea that this table, when no one is looking at it, either disappears or changes its shape & color, and only returns to its old state when someone looks at it? – “Who would even assume such a thing!” – one might say.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#7v.3+8r.1) <span class="fac">[7v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/7v.webp),[8r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-7v-3--8r-1}

Here it becomes clear that the concept of ‘agreement with reality’ is not entirely clear.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#8r.2) <span class="fac">[8r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-8r-2}

The sentence “It is written.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#8r.3+8v.1) <span class="fac">[8r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8r.webp),[8v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-8r-3--8v-1}

Who would assume that _all_ our calculations are unreliable, & that we cannot rely on any of them (with the justification that errors are possible everywhere), we would perhaps declare to be mad. But can we say that he is mistaken? Is he not simply reacting differently: we rely on them, he does not; we are certain, he is not.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#8v.2) <span class="fac">[8v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-8v-2}

Can I, for a moment, believe that I have ever been in the stratosphere? No. That is how I _know_ the contrary – like Moore?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#8v.3+9r.1) <span class="fac">[8v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/8v.webp),[9r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-8v-3--9r-1}

There can be no doubt about this for me, as a reasonable person. – That is what it is. –


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#9r.2) <span class="fac">[9r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-9r-2}

A reasonable person has certain doubts _not_.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#9r.3) <span class="fac">[9r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-9r-3}

Can I doubt what I _want_ to doubt?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#9r.4) <span class="fac">[9r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-9r-4}

I cannot possibly doubt that I was never in the stratosphere. Do I know it because of that, is that why it is true?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#9v.1) <span class="fac">[9v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-9v-1}

Could it not be that I am simply crazy and do not doubt what I absolutely should doubt.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#9v.2+10r.1) <span class="fac">[9v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/9v.webp),[10r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-9v-2--10r-1}

“I _know_ that it never happened, because if it had happened, I would have been unable to forget it.” But suppose it did happen, then you would have forgotten it. And how do you know that you could not possibly have forgotten it? Not just from past experience?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10r.2) <span class="fac">[10r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10r-2}

What I hold on to is not _a_ sentence, but a nest of sentences.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10r.3) <span class="fac">[10r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10r-3}

Can I subject the assumption that I was once on the moon to serious consideration at all?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10r.4) <span class="fac">[10r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10r-4}

“Is this something that one can forget?!”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10r.5+10v.1) <span class="fac">[10r\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10r.webp),[10v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10r-5--10v-1}

“Under such circumstances, people do not say: ‘Perhaps we have all forgotten it’ and so on, but they assume…”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10v.2) <span class="fac">[10v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10v-2}

Our language acquires its sense through our other actions.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#10v.3+11r.1) <span class="fac">[10v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/10v.webp),[11r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/11r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-10v-3--11r-1}

We ask ourselves: What do we do with the statement “I _know_…?” Because we are not concerned with processes or states of the mind. And _that_ is how one must decide whether something is knowledge or not.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#11r.2) <span class="fac">[11r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/11r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-11r-2}

If someone doubted whether the Earth existed 100 years ago, I wouldn’t understand _why_, because I wouldn’t know what he would still consider evidence, & what he wouldn’t.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#11r.3+11v.1) <span class="fac">[11r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/11r.webp),[11v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/11v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-11r-3--11v-1}

“Each of these facts we could doubt, but we cannot doubt _all_ of them.” Would it not be more correct to say: “We do not doubt _all_ of them.” The fact that we do not doubt them all is precisely the way we judge, i.e., act.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#11v.2+12r.1) <span class="fac">[11v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/11v.webp),[12r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/12r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-11v-2--12r-1}

If a child asked me whether the Earth existed before my birth, I would not answer that the Earth only came into existence with my birth, but that it existed long, long before. And in doing so, I would have the feeling of saying something strange. Something like when the child asked whether this or that mountain is higher than a very tall building that it has seen. I could only answer that question to someone to whom I would first impart a worldview. If I now answer the question with certainty, what gives me this certainty?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#12r.2+12v.1+13r.1) <span class="fac">[12r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/12r.webp),[12v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/12v.webp),[13r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/13r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-12r-2--12v-1--13r-1}

I believe that I have ancestors and that every human being has them. I believe that there are different cities and, in general, I believe in the main data of geography and history. I believe that the Earth is a body on whose surface we move and that it will not suddenly disappear or anything of the kind, like any other solid body: this table, this house, this tree, etc. If I wanted to doubt the existence of the Earth long before my birth, I would have to doubt everything that is certain to me.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#13r.2) <span class="fac">[13r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/13r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-13r-2}

And the fact that something is certain to me has its reason not in my stupidity or credulity.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#13r.3) <span class="fac">[13r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/13r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-13r-3}

If someone said “The Earth has not existed for a long time…” – what would he be touching upon? Do I know it?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#13v.1) <span class="fac">[13v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/13v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-13v-1}

Must it be a kind of scientific belief? Could it not be a mystical one? Must it necessarily contradict historical facts? Yes, even geographical ones?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#13v.2+14r.1+14v.1) <span class="fac">[13v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/13v.webp),[14r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/14r.webp),[14v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/14v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-13v-2--14r-1--14v-1}

If I say, “This table didn’t exist an hour ago,” I probably mean that it was only made later. If I say, “This mountain didn’t exist back then,” I presumably mean that it only formed later, perhaps volcanically. If I say, “This mountain didn’t exist half an hour ago,” that is such an odd statement that it is not clear what I mean. For example, whether I mean something false, but scientific. Perhaps one means that the statement that the mountain did not exist at that time is quite clear, however one imagines the connection. But imagine someone said, “This mountain didn’t exist a minute ago, but rather a perfectly identical one.” Only the familiar surroundings make it appear clear what is meant.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#14v.2+15r.1) <span class="fac">[14v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/14v.webp),[15r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/15r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-14v-2--15r-1}

So I could ask the person who claims that the Earth did not exist before his birth further questions in order to find out which of my opinions he contradicts. And it _might_ be that he contradicts my fundamental views. And if that were the case, then I would have to stop there. Similarly, if he says that he once was on the moon.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#15r.2+15v.1+16r.1) <span class="fac">[15r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/15r.webp),[15v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/15v.webp),[16r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/16r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-15r-2--15v-1--16r-1}

Yes, I believe that every human being has two human parents; but Catholics believe that Jesus only had one human mother. And others might believe that there are people who have no parents, and who do not believe in any contrary evidence. Catholics also believe that a wafer completely changes its nature under certain circumstances, and at the same time, that all evidence proves the opposite. So if M. said, “I know that this is wine and not blood,” Catholics would contradict him.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#16r.2+16v.1) <span class="fac">[16r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/16r.webp),[16v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/16v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-16r-2--16v-1}

What is the basis of the belief that all people have parents? Experience. And how can I base this firm belief on my experience? Well, I don’t just base it on the fact that I knew the parents of certain people, but on everything I have learned about the sex life of people, and their anatomy and physiology; also on what I have heard and seen about animals. But is that really proof?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#16v.2) <span class="fac">[16v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/16v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-16v-2}

Isn’t this a hypothesis which, as I _believe_, is always completely confirmed?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#16v.3) <span class="fac">[16v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/16v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-16v-3}

Shouldn’t we say, at every turn:

“I _believe_ this with certainty”?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#17r.1) <span class="fac">[17r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/17r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-17r-1}

“I know…” one says when one is ready to give compelling reasons. “I know” refers to a possibility of interpreting the truth. Whether someone knows something can be shown, assuming that he is convinced of it. But if what he believes is of such a nature that the reasons he can give are no more certain than his assertion, then he cannot say that he knows what he believes.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#17v.1) <span class="fac">[17v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/17v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-17v-1}

If someone says, “I have a body,” one can ask him, “Who is speaking here with this mouth?”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#17v.2+18r.1+18v.1) <span class="fac">[17v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/17v.webp),[18r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/18r.webp),[18v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/18v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-17v-2--18r-1--18v-1}

To whom does one say that one knows something? To oneself, or to another. If one says it to oneself, how does it differ from stating that one is _certain_ that it is so? There is no subjective certainty that I know something. Subjective is the certainty, but not the knowledge. If I therefore say “I know that I have two hands” & that is not just supposed to express my subjective certainty, then I must be able to prove to myself that I am right. But I cannot; for the fact that I have two hands is no less certain before I have looked at them than afterwards.

However, I could say: “The fact that I have two hands is an unshakable belief.” That would express that I am not prepared to accept anything as a counter-proof of this sentence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#18v.2) <span class="fac">[18v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/18v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-18v-2}

“Here I have arrived at a basis for all my knowledge.” “I will _maintain_ this position!” But isn’t that precisely because I am completely _convinced_ of it? – What does it mean to ‘be completely convinced’?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#18v.3+19r.1) <span class="fac">[18v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/18v.webp),[19r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-18v-3--19r-1}

What if I were to doubt now that I have two hands? Why can I not even imagine it?

What would I believe if I did not believe that? I don’t even have a system in which this doubt could exist.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#19r.2) <span class="fac">[19r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-19r-2}

I have arrived at the ground of my convictions.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#19r.3+19v.1) <span class="fac">[19r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19r.webp),[19v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-19r-3--19v-1}

And one could almost say that this foundation is what supports the whole house.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#19v.2) <span class="fac">[19v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-19v-2}

One forms a false picture of _doubt_.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#19v.3) <span class="fac">[19v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-19v-3}

That I have two hands is, under normal circumstances, as certain as anything I could offer as evidence for it. Therefore, I am unable to regard the sight of my hand as evidence for it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#19v.4+20r.1) <span class="fac">[19v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/19v.webp),[20r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-19v-4--20r-1}

Does this not mean: I will act according to this belief without fail & will not allow myself to be swayed by anything?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#20r.2) <span class="fac">[20r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-20r-2}

But it is not only that _I_ believe in this way that I have 2 hands, but that every reasonable person does.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#20r.3) <span class="fac">[20r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-20r-3}

At the root of justified belief lies unjustified belief.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#20r.4+20v.1) <span class="fac">[20r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20r.webp),[20v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-20r-4--20v-1}

Every ‘reasonable’ person acts _like this_.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#20v.2+21r.1) <span class="fac">[20v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/20v.webp),[21r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/21r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-20v-2--21r-1}

Doubt has certain characteristic manifestations, but they are only characteristic of him under certain circumstances. If someone said that he doubted the existence of his hands, kept looking at them from all sides, and tried to convince himself that there was no reflection or the like, then we would not be sure whether we should call that doubt. We could describe his way of acting as similar to that of a doubter, but his game would not be ours.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#21r.2) <span class="fac">[21r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/21r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-21r-2}

On the other hand, the language-game changes with time.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#21r.3+21v.1) <span class="fac">[21r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/21r.webp),[21v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/21v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-21r-3--21v-1}

If someone told me that he doubted whether he had a body, I would consider him half crazy. But I would not know what it would mean to convince him that he did have one. And if I had said something that now resolved the doubt, I would not know how and why.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#21v.2+22r.1) <span class="fac">[21v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/21v.webp),[22r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/22r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-21v-2--22r-1}

I do not know how the sentence “I have a body” is to be used. This does not necessarily apply to the sentence that I have always been on or near the Earth.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#22r.2) <span class="fac">[22r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/22r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-22r-2}

Someone who doubted that the Earth has existed for 100 years could have a scientific or a philosophical doubt.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#22r.3) <span class="fac">[22r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/22r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-22r-3}

I would like to reserve the expression “I know” for the cases in which it is used in normal language.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#22v.1) <span class="fac">[22v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/22v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-22v-1}

I cannot imagine a reasonable doubt about the existence of the Earth during the last 100 years.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#22v.2+23r.1) <span class="fac">[22v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/22v.webp),[23r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/23r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-22v-2--23r-1}

I can imagine a person who grew up under very special circumstances and was taught that the Earth came into being 50 years ago, and who therefore also believes it. We could instruct this person: the Earth has been around for a long time, etc. – We would try to give him our worldview. This would be done through a kind of _persuasion_.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#23r.2) <span class="fac">[23r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/23r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-23r-2}

The student _believes_ his teachers and the textbooks.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#23r.3+23v.1) <span class="fac">[23r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/23r.webp),[23v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/23v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-23r-3--23v-1}

I can imagine the case that M. is captured by a wild tribe and they express the suspicion that he comes from somewhere between the Earth and the Moon. M. tells them that he knows, …, but he cannot give them the reasons for his certainty, because they have fantastic ideas about a person’s ability to fly and know nothing about physics. This would be an occasion to make that statement.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#23v.2+24r.1) <span class="fac">[23v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/23v.webp),[24r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-23v-2--24r-1}

But what does it say more than “I have never been there and there, and I have compelling reasons to believe that”?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#24r.2) <span class="fac">[24r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-24r-2}

And here one would still have to say what compelling reasons are.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#24r.3) <span class="fac">[24r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-24r-3}

“I not only have a visual impression of a tree, but I _know_ that it is a tree.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#24r.4+24v.1) <span class="fac">[24r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24r.webp),[24v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-24r-4--24v-1}

“I know that this is a hand.” – And what is a hand? – “Well, _that_ for example.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#24v.2) <span class="fac">[24v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-24v-2}

Am I more certain that I have never been to the moon than that I have never been to Bulgaria? Why am I so sure? Well, I know that I have also never been anywhere near it, e.g. never in the Balkans.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#24v.3) <span class="fac">[24v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/24v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-24v-3}

“I have compelling reasons for my certainty.” These reasons make the certainty objective.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#25r.1) <span class="fac">[25r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-25r-1}

What constitutes a valid reason for something is not decided by me.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#25r.2) <span class="fac">[25r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-25r-2}

I know = It is certainly known to me.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#25r.3) <span class="fac">[25r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-25r-3}

When, however, does one say that something is certain? Because one can argue about whether something _is_ certain; namely, if something is _objectively_ certain.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#25r.4+25v.1) <span class="fac">[25r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25r.webp),[25v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-25r-4--25v-1}

There are countless general sentences of experience that are considered certain by us.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#25v.2+26r.1) <span class="fac">[25v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/25v.webp),[26r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/26r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-25v-2--26r-1}

That if one has an arm chopped off, it will not grow back, is one such. That if one has one’s head chopped off, one is dead and will never be alive again, is another. One can say that experience teaches us these sentences. However, it does not teach them to us in isolation, but it teaches us a whole series of related sentences. If they were isolated, I could, for example, doubt them, because I have no experience concerning them.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#26r.2+26v.1) <span class="fac">[26r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/26r.webp),[26v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/26v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-26r-2--26v-1}

Is experience the basis of this certainty of ours, then it is naturally past experience. And it is not merely _my_ experience, but that of others, of which I become aware. Now one could say that it is once again experience that makes us trust others. But what experience makes me believe that the books on anatomy & physiology do not contain falsehoods? It is probably true that this trust is also _supported_ by my own experience.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#26v.2+27r.1) <span class="fac">[26v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/26v.webp),[27r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/27r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-26v-2--27r-1}

We believe, so to speak, that this large building is there, & now we see a little corner here, a little corner there.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#27r.2) <span class="fac">[27r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/27r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-27r-2}

“I cannot help but believe …”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#27r.3) <span class="fac">[27r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/27r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-27r-3}

“I am reassured that this is so.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#27r.4+27v.1+28r.1) <span class="fac">[27r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/27r.webp),[27v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/27v.webp),[28r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/28r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-27r-4--27v-1--28r-1}

It is quite certain that automobiles do not grow out of the ground. – We feel that if one were to believe the opposite, one could give credence to _everything_ we declare to be impossible & dispute everything we consider certain. But how is this _one_ belief connected with all the others? We would like to say that whoever can believe that does not accept the entire system of our verification. This system is something that a person acquires through observation & instruction. I intentionally say not “learns.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#28r.2) <span class="fac">[28r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/28r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-28r-2}

After he has seen this & that & heard this & that, he is unable to doubt that …


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#28r.3+28v.1) <span class="fac">[28r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/28r.webp),[28v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/28v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-28r-3--28v-1}

_I_, L.W., believe, am certain that my friend does not have sawdust in his stomach or head, although I have no direct sensory evidence for it. I am certain, based on what was told to me, what I read, & my experiences. To doubt this seems to me like madness, of course, again in agreement with others; but _I_ agree with them.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#28v.2+29r.1) <span class="fac">[28v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/28v.webp),[29r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/29r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-28v-2--29r-1}

I cannot say that I have good reasons for the view that cats do not grow on trees, or that I had a father & a mother. If someone doubts this, – how could it have happened?

Should he never have believed from the beginning that he had parents? But is that conceivable, unless someone taught him this?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#29r.2+29v.1) <span class="fac">[29r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/29r.webp),[29v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/29v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-29r-2--29v-1}

For how can the child doubt what is taught to him? That could only mean that it could not learn certain language-games.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#29v.2+30r.1) <span class="fac">[29v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/29v.webp),[30r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/30r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-29v-2--30r-1}

People have been killing animals since the oldest times, using their fur, their bones, etc., for certain purposes; they certainly expected to find similar parts in each similar animal. They have always learned from experience, & from their actions one can see that they certainly believed in certain things, whether they expressed this belief or not. Of course, I do not mean to say that people should act in this way, but only that they act in this way.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#30r.2) <span class="fac">[30r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/30r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-30r-2}

If someone is looking for something & digs up the earth at a certain place, he shows by that that he believes that what he is looking for is there.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#30v.1+31r.1) <span class="fac">[30v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/30v.webp),[31r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/31r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-30v-1--31r-1}

What we believe depends on what we learn. We all believe that it is impossible to get to the moon; but there might be people who believe that it is possible & happens sometimes. We say: these do not know much of what we know. And they may be ever so sure of their case – they are in error, & we know it. If we compare our system of knowledge with theirs, theirs is revealed as the much poorer one.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#31r.2) <span class="fac">[31r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/31r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-31r-2}

23.09.1950

The squirrel does not conclude by induction that it will also need supplies in the next winter. And we do not need a law of induction to justify our actions or predictions.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#31r.3+31v.1+32r.1) <span class="fac">[31r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/31r.webp),[31v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/31v.webp),[32r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/32r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-31r-3--31v-1--32r-1}

I not only know that the earth existed long before my birth, but also that it is a large body, that this has been established, that I & the other people have many ancestors, that there are books about all this, that such books do not lie, etc., etc., etc.. And do I know all this? I believe it. This body of knowledge has been passed on to me & I have no reason to doubt it, but rather have many confirmations. And why should I not say that I know all this?

Isn't that what one says? But not only do I know, or believe all this, but so do the others. Or rather, I _believe_ that they believe it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#32r.2) <span class="fac">[32r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/32r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-32r-2}

I am firmly convinced that the others believe, believe that everything is as it is.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#32r.3+32v.1) <span class="fac">[32r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/32r.webp),[32v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/32v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-32r-3--32v-1}

I myself wrote in my book that a child learns to understand a word in such and such a way. Do I know this, or do I believe it? Why in such a case do I not write “I believe…”, but simply the assertive sentence?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#32v.2+33r.1) <span class="fac">[32v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/32v.webp),[33r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-32v-2--33r-1}

We know that the earth is round. We have finally become convinced that it is round. We will stick to this view, unless our entire view of nature changes. “How do you know that?” – I believe it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#33r.2) <span class="fac">[33r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-33r-2}

Further attempts cannot _rectify the earlier ones_, at most they can alter our entire consideration.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#33r.3) <span class="fac">[33r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-33r-3}

Similarly, the sentence “Water boils at 100˚ C.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#33r.4+33v.1) <span class="fac">[33r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33r.webp),[33v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-33r-4--33v-1}

_Thus_, we convince ourselves; _that_ is what one calls “being rightly convinced of it”.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#33v.2) <span class="fac">[33v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-33v-2}

Doesn’t one also, in this sense, have a _proof_ of the proposition? But it is not a proof that the same thing will happen again; but we say that it gives us a right to assume this.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#33v.3) <span class="fac">[33v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/33v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-33v-3}

This is what we _call_ “the empirical justification of our assumptions.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#34r.1) <span class="fac">[34r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/34r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-34r-1}

We do not just learn that the such and such experiments turned out so and so, but also the concluding sentence. And there is, of course, nothing wrong with this. Because this sentence is an instrument for a certain use.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#34r.2+34v.1) <span class="fac">[34r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/34r.webp),[34v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/34v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-34r-2--34v-1}

We are quite certain of this, which does not only mean that each individual is certain of it, but that we belong to a community that is connected through science and education.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#34v.2) <span class="fac">[34v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/34v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-34v-2}

We are satisfied that the earth is round.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#35r.2) <span class="fac">[35r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/35r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-35r-2}

Not all corrections of our views are on the same level.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#35r.3+35v.1) <span class="fac">[35r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/35r.webp),[35v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/35v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-35r-3--35v-1}

Suppose it is not true that the earth existed long before my birth, how is one to imagine the discovery of this error?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#35v.2) <span class="fac">[35v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/35v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-35v-2}

It is of no use to say “Perhaps we are mistaken” if, when _no one_ trusts the evidence, one does not trust the evidence in the case of the present evidence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#35v.3) <span class="fac">[35v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/35v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-35v-3}

If we have always miscalculated and 12 × 12 is not 144, why should we then trust any other calculation? And this is, of course, poorly expressed.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#36r.1) <span class="fac">[36r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-36r-1}

But I also do not make a mistake in this formula of the multiplication table. I may later say that I was confused, but not that I was mistaken.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#36r.2) <span class="fac">[36r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-36r-2}

Here, another step is needed, similar to the one in the theory of relativity.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#36r.3) <span class="fac">[36r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-36r-3}

“I don’t know if this is a hand…” But do you know what the word “hand” means? And don’t say “I know what it means to me now.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#36v.1) <span class="fac">[36v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/36v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-36v-1}

And isn't this an empirical fact that _this_ word is used _in this way_?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#36v.2) <span class="fac">[36v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/36v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-36v-2}

And here it is peculiar that, even if I am quite certain of the _use_ of the words, have no doubt about it, I cannot give any _reasons_ for my way of acting. If I were to try, I could give 1000, but none of them would be as certain as the things that they are supposed to justify.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#37r.1+37v.1) <span class="fac">[37r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/37r.webp),[37v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/37v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-37r-1--37v-1}

‘Knowledge’ & ‘certainty’ belong to different _categories_. They are not two ‘states of mind’ like, for example, ‘suspecting’ & ‘being certain’. (Here, I assume that it is meaningful for me to say, “I know what the word ‘doubt’ (for example) means, & that this sentence assigns a logical role to the word ‘doubt.’”) What interests us now is not certainty, but knowledge. That is to say, we are interested in the fact that there can be no doubt about certain experiential propositions if judgment is to be possible at all. Or also: I am inclined to believe that not everything that has the form of an experiential proposition is an experiential proposition.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#37v.2) <span class="fac">[37v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/37v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-37v-2}

Is it that the rule and the empirical sentence merge into each other?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#37v.3+38r.1) <span class="fac">[37v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/37v.webp),[38r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-37v-3--38r-1}

A student and a teacher. The student does not let anything be explained to him, because he constantly interrupts (the teacher) with doubts, e.g. about the existence of things, the meaning of the words, etc.. The teacher says: “Don’t interrupt anymore and do what I tell you. Your doubts don’t make any sense yet.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#38r.2) <span class="fac">[38r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-38r-2}

Or imagine, the student doubted the story (and everything connected with it), yes, even whether the Earth existed 100 years ago.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#38r.3+38v.1) <span class="fac">[38r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38r.webp),[38v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-38r-3--38v-1}

It seems to me as if this doubt is hollow. But isn't it also the _belief_ in the story? No; it is connected with so much.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#38v.2) <span class="fac">[38v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-38v-2}

So, is _that_ what makes us believe a sentence? Well, it is the case that the grammar of “believe” is connected with that of the sentence being believed.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#38v.3+39r.1) <span class="fac">[38v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/38v.webp),[39r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/39r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-38v-3--39r-1}

Imagine, the student really asked: “And is a table also there when I turn around; and even if _no one_ sees it?” Should the teacher reassure him? and say “Of course it is there!” – Perhaps the teacher will become a little impatient, thinking that the student will eventually stop asking such questions.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#39r.2+39v.1+40r.1) <span class="fac">[39r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/39r.webp),[39v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/39v.webp),[40r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-39r-2--39v-1--40r-1}

That is, the teacher will feel that this is not really a legitimate question. And the same applies if the student doubted the lawfulness of nature, i.e., the justification for induction. – The teacher would feel that this would only hold him and the student back, that he would only get stuck in learning and not make progress. – And he would be right. It would be as if someone were looking for an object in the room; he opens a drawer and does not see it there; he closes it again, waits, and opens it again to see if it is not there now, and so on. He has not yet learned how to search. And so, that student has not yet learned how to ask questions. Not learned the _game_ that we want to teach him.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#40r.2) <span class="fac">[40r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-40r-2}

And is it not the same as when the student interrupts the history lesson by doubting whether the Earth really…?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#40r.3+40v.1) <span class="fac">[40r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40r.webp),[40v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-40r-3--40v-1}

This doubt does not belong to the doubts of our game. (But not as if we were choosing this game!)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#40v.2) <span class="fac">[40v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-40v-2}

12.03.1951

‘The question does not arise at all.’ Your answer would characterize a _method_. But there is no sharp boundary between methodological sentences and sentences within a method.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#40v.3+41r.1) <span class="fac">[40v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/40v.webp),[41r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-40v-3--41r-1}

But could one then not say that there is no sharp boundary between sentences of logic and empirical sentences? The vagueness is precisely that of the boundary between _rule_ and empirical sentence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#41r.2) <span class="fac">[41r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-41r-2}

Here, I think, one must remember that the concept of ‘sentence’ itself is not sharp.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#41r.3+41v.1) <span class="fac">[41r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41r.webp),[41v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-41r-3--41v-1}

I say: Every empirical sentence can be transformed into a postulate – and then becomes a norm of representation. But I also have a suspicion about that. The sentence is too general.

One might almost say “Every empirical sentence can, in theory, be transformed…”, but what does “in theory” mean here? It just sounds too much like the Log. Phil. Abh.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#41v.2) <span class="fac">[41v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-41v-2}

How, if the student did not want to believe that this mountain has stood there since time immemorial? We would say that he has no _reason_ for this distrust.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#41v.3+42r.1) <span class="fac">[41v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/41v.webp),[42r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-41v-3--42r-1}

So, must reasonable distrust have a reason? We could also say: “The reasonable person believes this.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#42r.2) <span class="fac">[42r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-42r-2}

So we would not call someone reasonable who believes something, despite scientific evidence.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#42r.3+42v.1) <span class="fac">[42r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42r.webp),[42v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-42r-3--42v-1}

If we say that we _know_ that…, we mean that every reasonable person in our situation would also know it, that it would be unreasonable to doubt it. Moore also does not only want to say that _he_ knows that he etc. etc., but also that every person with reasonable faculties in his situation would know it just as well.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#42v.2) <span class="fac">[42v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-42v-2}

But who tells us what is reasonable to believe in _this_ situation?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#42v.3+43r.1) <span class="fac">[42v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/42v.webp),[43r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-42v-3--43r-1}

One could therefore say: “The reasonable person believes: that the Earth existed long before his birth, that his life took place on the Earth's surface or not far from it, that he has, for example, never been on the moon, that he has a nervous system and various internal organs like all other people, etc. etc.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#43r.2) <span class="fac">[43r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-43r-2}

‘I know it _in_ the same way that I know that I am called L.W.’


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#43r.3) <span class="fac">[43r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-43r-3}

‘If he _doubts_ this – whatever “doubts” means here – then he will never learn this game.’


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#43r.4+43v.1) <span class="fac">[43r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43r.webp),[43v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-43r-4--43v-1}

The sentence “I know…” therefore expresses here the willingness to believe certain things.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#43v.2) <span class="fac">[43v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-43v-2}

13.03.1951

If we act with certainty, should we then be surprised that we cannot doubt many things?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#43v.3+44r.1) <span class="fac">[43v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/43v.webp),[44r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/44r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-43v-3--44r-1}

Imagine someone, without wanting to _philosophize_, says: “I don’t know if I’ve ever been to the moon; I don’t _remember_ ever having been there.” (Why would this person be so fundamentally different from us?) Above all: How would he know that he is on the moon? How does he imagine it? Consider: “I don’t know if I’ve ever been to village X.” But I couldn’t say that either if X were in Turkey, because I know that I’ve never been to Turkey.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#44r.2+44v.1) <span class="fac">[44r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/44r.webp),[44v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/44v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-44r-2--44v-1}

I ask someone: “Have you ever been to China?” He replies: “I don’t know.” One would then say: “You don’t _know_ it? Do you have any reason to believe that you might have been there at some point? For example, were you ever near the Chinese border? Or were your parents there at the time you were born?” – Usually, Europeans know whether or not they have been to China.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#44v.2) <span class="fac">[44v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/44v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-44v-2}

D.h.: the rational person only doubts _that_ under certain circumstances.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#44v.3+45r.1) <span class="fac">[44v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/44v.webp),[45r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/45r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-44v-3--45r-1}

The procedure in a courtroom is based on the fact that circumstances give statements a certain probability. The statement, for example, that someone was born without parents, would never be considered there.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#45r.2+45v.1) <span class="fac">[45r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/45r.webp),[45v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/45v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-45r-2--45v-1}

But what seems reasonable or unreasonable to people changes. At certain times, something seems reasonable to people that seemed unreasonable at other times. And vice versa. But isn't there an objective criterion here? _Very_ clever & educated people believe in the creation story of the Bible, & others consider it demonstrably false, & these reasons are known to them.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#45v.2+46r.1) <span class="fac">[45v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/45v.webp),[46r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/46r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-45v-2--46r-1}

One cannot experiment if one does not doubt certain things. But that does not mean that one then accepts certain presuppositions on good faith. If I write & mail a letter, I assume that it will arrive; that is what I expect. When I experiment, I do not doubt the existence of the apparatus that I have before my eyes. I have many doubts, but not _that one_. If I do a calculation, I believe, without a doubt, that the figures on the paper do not change themselves, & I also continuously trust my memory & absolutely rely on it. Here, it is the same certainty as the fact that I have not been to the moon.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#46r.2+46v.1+47r.1) <span class="fac">[46r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/46r.webp),[46v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/46v.webp),[47r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/47r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-46r-2--46v-1--47r-1}

Let us imagine people who would never be quite certain about these things, but who would probably say that it is _very_ likely so & that it is not worth doubting. Such a person, if he were in my position, would say: “It is highly improbable that I have ever been on the moon”, etc., etc. _How_ would the life of these people differ from ours? There are people who say that it is only highly improbable that the water in the kettle, which is over the fire, will boil & not freeze; it is (therefore) strictly speaking only improbable, what we regard as impossible. What difference does this make in their life? Is it not only that they talk a little more about certain things than the others?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#47r.2+47v.1) <span class="fac">[47r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/47r.webp),[47v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/47v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-47r-2--47v-1}

Imagine a person who is supposed to pick up his friend from the train station, & instead of simply checking the timetable & going to the station at the specified time, he says: “I don’t _really_ believe the train will arrive, but I will still go to the station.” He does everything that an ordinary person would do, but accompanies it with doubts or a lack of will regarding himself, etc.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#47v.2+48r.1) <span class="fac">[47v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/47v.webp),[48r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/48r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-47v-2--48r-1}

With the same certainty with which we believe _any_ mathematical sentence, we also know how the letters “A” & “B” are pronounced, what the color of human blood is called, that other people have blood & call it “blood”.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#48r.2) <span class="fac">[48r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/48r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-48r-2}

In other words, the _questions_ we pose & our _doubts_ rest on the unquestionability of those assumptions, which are, as it were, the anchors around which those doubts revolve.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#48v.1) <span class="fac">[48v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/48v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-48v-1}

That is to say, it is part of the logic of our scientific investigations that certain things are not doubted, _indeed_.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#48v.2) <span class="fac">[48v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/48v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-48v-2}

However, it is not the case that we simply _cannot_ investigate everything: & that we must therefore reluctantly be satisfied with an assumption. If I want the door to turn, the hinges must be firmly in place.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#48v.3) <span class="fac">[48v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/48v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-48v-3}

My _life_ consists in the fact that one is content with many things.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#49r.1) <span class="fac">[49r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/49r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-49r-1}

If I ask, “What color do you see now,” in order to find out what color is there now, then I cannot at the same time also doubt whether the person addressed understands German, whether he wants to deceive me, whether my own memory is not failing me with regard to the meaning of color names, etc.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#49r.2+49v.1) <span class="fac">[49r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/49r.webp),[49v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/49v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-49r-2--49v-1}

If I am trying to checkmate someone in chess, I cannot doubt whether the pieces might not change their positions on their own and at the same time my memory is playing tricks on me, so that I don't notice.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#49v.2+50r.1) <span class="fac">[49v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/49v.webp),[50r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/50r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-49v-2--50r-1}

15.03.1951

“I know that that is a tree.” Why does it seem to me as if I don’t understand the sentence, even though it is a very simple sentence, of the most ordinary kind? It is as if I cannot set my mind to any meaning. Because I am not looking for the meaning in the area where it is. As soon as I think of a common application of the sentence, as opposed to a philosophical one, its sense becomes clear and ordinary.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#50r.2+50v.1) <span class="fac">[50r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/50r.webp),[50v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/50v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-50r-2--50v-1}

Just as the words “I am here” only have sense in certain contexts, but not if I say them to someone who is sitting opposite me and can clearly see me – and not because they are then superfluous, but because their sense is not _determined_ by the situation, but such a determination is needed.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#50v.2+51r.1+51v.1) <span class="fac">[50v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/50v.webp),[51r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/51r.webp),[51v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/51v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-50v-2--51r-1--51v-1}

“I know that that is a tree” – this can mean all sorts of things: I am looking at a plant that I take to be a young beech tree, and the other person takes it to be a currant bush. He says “That is a bush,” I say it is a tree. – We see something in the fog, which one of us thinks is a human being, the other says “I know that that is a tree.” Someone wants to test my eyes, etc., etc. – Every time, the “that” that I declare to be a tree is of a different kind. But what if we were to express ourselves more precisely? for example: “I know that that tree over there is a tree, I can see it clearly enough.” – Let us even assume that I made this remark in the context of a conversation (which was therefore relevant at the time); and now, out of all context, I repeat it, while looking at the tree, and I add “I mean these words in the same way as 5 minutes ago.” – If, for example, I added that I was thinking of my poor eyesight and it was a kind of sigh, then there would be nothing mysterious about the utterance. How the sentence is _meant_ can be expressed by an addition to the sentence and can thus be reconciled with it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#51v.2+52r.1+52v.1+53r.1) <span class="fac">[51v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/51v.webp),[52r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/52r.webp),[52v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/52v.webp),[53r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/53r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-51v-2--52r-1--52v-1--53r-1}

“I know that that is a tree” – a philosopher might say this in order to bring before his own eyes or the eyes of another that he _knows_ something that is not a mathematical or logical truth. Similarly, someone who is preoccupied with the thought that he is no longer of use to anyone might repeatedly say to himself, “I can still do this and that.” If such thoughts were to occur more often in his head, one would not be surprised if he spoke such a sentence to himself, apparently out of all context. (But I have already sketched a background, an environment for these utterances, thus giving them a context.) If, on the other hand, someone were to exclaim, with the most convincing mime, under completely heterogeneous circumstances, “Down with him!” one could say of these words (and their tone) that they are a figure, which certainly has well-known applications, but here it is not even clear which _language_ the person is speaking. I could make with my hand the movement that would be made if I had a fox's tail in my hand and was sawing a board; but would one be justified in calling this movement a _sawing_ movement outside of all context? (It could be something completely different!)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#53r.2+53v.1) <span class="fac">[53r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/53r.webp),[53v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/53v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-53r-2--53v-1}

Isn't the question “Do these words have sense” similar to the question: “Is this a tool,” when one shows, say, a hammer. I say “Yes, that is a hammer.” But what if what each of us considers to be a hammer were something else, e.g., a projectile, or a conductor's baton? Now, let's perform the application ourselves!


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#53v.2+54r.1) <span class="fac">[53v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/53v.webp),[54r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/54r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-53v-2--54r-1}

Now, if someone says “I know that that is a tree,” I can reply: “Yes, that is a sentence. A German sentence. And what is the point of it?” If he then replies: “I just wanted to remind myself that I _know_ something like that”? – – –


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#54r.2) <span class="fac">[54r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/54r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-54r-2}

But how if he said: “I want to make a logical remark”? – – – When the forester goes into the forest with his workers & now says “_This_ tree is to be cut down, & _this_ & _this_” – – – how if he makes the remark “I _know_ that that is a tree”? – Couldn’t one say of the forester, “He _knows_ that that is a tree; he isn’t investigating it, he isn’t ordering his men to investigate it.”?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#54v.1) <span class="fac">[54v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/54v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-54v-1}

Doubtful and non-doubtful behavior. The former exists only if the latter exists.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#54v.2+t55r.1) <span class="fac">[54v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/54v.webp),[t55r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/t55r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-54v-2--t55r-1}

The psychiatrist, for example, might ask me, “Do you know what this is,” & I might answer, “I know that this is a chair; I am familiar with it; it has always been in my room.” He might not be testing my eyesight there, but rather my ability to recognize things, to know their name & their function. This is about being familiar with something. It would be wrong for me to say, “I think that this is a chair,” because that would express a willingness to have the statement tested. Whereas “I know that this is…” implies that _surprise_ would occur if confirmation did not occur.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#55r.2+55v.1) <span class="fac">[55r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/55r.webp),[55v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/55v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-55r-2--55v-1}

My ‘state of mind,’ this ‘knowledge,’ does not serve me well for what will happen. However, it consists in the fact that I would not understand where a doubt could arise, where a verification would be possible.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#55v.2) <span class="fac">[55v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/55v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-55v-2}

One could say: “ ‘I know’ expresses the _reassured_ certainty, not the one that is still struggling.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#55v.3) <span class="fac">[55v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/55v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-55v-3}

I would now like to see this certainty not as something related to rashness or superficiality, but as a form of life. (This is very poorly expressed and perhaps poorly thought out.)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#56r.1) <span class="fac">[56r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/56r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-56r-1}

That is, I want to understand it as something that lies beyond what is justified and unjustified; as something animalistic, so to speak.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#56r.3+56v.1) <span class="fac">[56r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/56r.webp),[56v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/56v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-56r-3--56v-1}

I _know_ that this is my foot. I could not accept any experience as proof of the contrary. – This could be an exclamation; but what _follows_ from it? In any case, that I will act in accordance with what I know with absolute certainty.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#56v.2+57r.1) <span class="fac">[56v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/56v.webp),[57r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-56v-2--57r-1}

However, I could also say that it has been revealed to me by God that this is the case. God has taught me that this is my foot. And if something were to happen that seems to contradict this knowledge, then I would have to regard _that_ as an illusion.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#57r.2) <span class="fac">[57r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-57r-2}

But doesn’t this show that knowledge is related to a decision?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#57r.3) <span class="fac">[57r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-57r-3}

And it is difficult here to find the transition from what one wants to exclaim to the practical consequences.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#57v.1) <span class="fac">[57v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/57v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-57v-1}

One could also ask: “If you know that that is your foot, do you know it, or do you only believe that no future experience will seem to contradict your knowledge?” (i.e., that it will not seem so to _you yourself_?)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#57v.2+58r.1) <span class="fac">[57v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/57v.webp),[58r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-57v-2--58r-1}

If someone were to reply: “I also know that it will never _seem_ to me as though something contradicts that insight,” what can we infer from this? Only that he himself does not doubt that it will never happen.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#58r.2) <span class="fac">[58r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-58r-2}

As if it were forbidden to say “I know” and only allowed to say “I believe to know”?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#58r.3+58v.1) <span class="fac">[58r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58r.webp),[58v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-58r-3--58v-1}

Isn’t the purpose of constructing a word like “know” analogously with “believe” that the statement “I know” is then associated with opprobrium if the person who says it is mistaken? An error is thereby made something impermissible.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#58v.2) <span class="fac">[58v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-58v-2}

If someone says that he will not accept any experience as proof of the contrary, then that is, after all, a _decision_. It is possible that he will act contrary to it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#58v.3+59r.1) <span class="fac">[58v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/58v.webp),[59r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/59r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-58v-3--59r-1}

March 16, 1951

If I wanted to doubt that this is my hand, how could I doubt that the word “hand” has some meaning? So it seems I do _know_ that.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#59r.2) <span class="fac">[59r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/59r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-59r-2}

Rather: That I use the word “hand” & all the other words in my sentence without reservation, yes, that I would be at a loss if I even tried to doubt it – shows that certainty is part of the essence of the language-game, that the question “How do I know…” takes the language-game beyond its limits, or dissolves it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#59v.1) <span class="fac">[59v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/59v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-59v-1}

Does “I know that that is a hand” in Moore’s sense mean the same or something similar to: I can use statements such as “I have pain in this hand,” or “This hand is weaker than the other,” or “I once broke this hand,” etc. – – –


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#60r.2) <span class="fac">[60r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/60r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-60r-2}

that I can use statements such as… in language-games in which a doubt about the existence of this hand does not arise.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#60r.3+60v.1) <span class="fac">[60r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/60r.webp),[60v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/60v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-60r-3--60v-1}

Only in certain cases is an investigation “Is this really a hand?” (or “my hand”) possible. For the sentence “I doubt whether this is really my (or a) hand” has no sense without further specification. It is not possible to discern from these words alone what kind of doubt is meant.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#60v.2) <span class="fac">[60v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/60v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-60v-2}

Why should it be possible to have a reason to _believe_ if it is not possible to be certain?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#60v.3+61r.1) <span class="fac">[60v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/60v.webp),[61r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/61r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-60v-3--61r-1}

We teach the child “This is your hand,” not “This is perhaps [or ‘probably’] your hand.” Thus the child learns the countless language-games that involve his hand. An investigation or question, “Is this really a hand?” does not even occur to him. On the other hand, he does not learn: he _knows_ that this is his hand.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#61r.2+61v.1) <span class="fac">[61r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/61r.webp),[61v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/61v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-61r-2--61v-1}

One must recognize here that complete certainty in one point, even where ‘justified’ doubts might exist, does not necessarily falsify a language-game. There is also such a thing as a _different_ kind of arithmetic. This admission, I believe, must lie at the root of all understanding of logic.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#61v.2) <span class="fac">[61v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/61v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-61v-2}

March 16, 1951

I can passionately affirm that I know that this (e.g.) is my foot.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#61v.3+62r.1) <span class="fac">[61v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/61v.webp),[62r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-61v-3--62r-1}

But this passion is something (very) rare, & there is no trace of it when I ordinarily speak of this foot.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#62r.2) <span class="fac">[62r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-62r-2}

Knowledge is ultimately based on recognition.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#62r.3) <span class="fac">[62r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-62r-3}
