I say passionately “I _know_ that that is a foot” – but what does it _mean_?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#62r.4+62v.1) <span class="fac">[62r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62r.webp),[62v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-62r-4--62v-1}

I could continue: “Nothing in the world will convince me of the contrary!” The fact is, at bottom, the foundation of all knowledge. I will give up other things, but not this.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#62v.2) <span class="fac">[62v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-62v-2}

This “Nothing in the world…” is obviously an attitude that one does not have toward everything one believes, or of which one is ‘certain’.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#62v.3+63r.1) <span class="fac">[62v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/62v.webp),[63r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-62v-3--63r-1}

This does not mean that nothing in the world is actually capable of convincing me of something else.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#63r.2) <span class="fac">[63r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-63r-2}

The argument “Perhaps I am dreaming” is therefore senseless, because then this utterance too is dreamed, yes, even _that_ the words have meaning.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#63r.3) <span class="fac">[63r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-63r-3}

What kind of statement is “Nothing in the world…”?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#63r.4+63v.1) <span class="fac">[63r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63r.webp),[63v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-63r-4--63v-1}

It has the form of a prediction, but is (of course) not a prediction based on experience.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#63v.3+64r.1) <span class="fac">[63v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/63v.webp),[64r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/64r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-63v-3--64r-1}

Whoever, like Moore, says that he _knows_ that… indicates the degree of certainty that something has for him. And it is important that there is a maximum for this degree.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#64r.2+64v.1) <span class="fac">[64r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/64r.webp),[64v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/64v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-64r-2--64v-1}

One could ask me: “How certain are you that that is a tree; that you have money in your pocket; that that is your foot?” And the answer could be “not certain” in one case, “almost certain” in another, “I cannot doubt it” in the third. And these answers would make sense even without any reasons. I do not need to say, for example, “I cannot be sure whether that is a tree, because my eyes are not sharp enough.” I want to say: it made sense for Moore to say “I _know_ that that is a tree” if he meant to say something quite specific.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#64v.2+65r.1) <span class="fac">[64v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/64v.webp),[65r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/65r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-64v-2--65r-1}

I believe a philosopher, one who can think for himself, might be interested in reading my notes. Because even if I have only rarely hit the mark, he would still recognize the goals towards which I have tirelessly aimed.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#65r.2+65v.1) <span class="fac">[65r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/65r.webp),[65v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/65v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-65r-2--65v-1}

Each of us often uses such a sentence, and it is not questionable whether it has sense. But can a philosophical insight also be derived from it? Is it more a proof of the existence of external things that I know that this is a hand, than that I do not know whether it is gold or brass?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#65v.2+66r.1) <span class="fac">[65v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/65v.webp),[66r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/66r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-65v-2--66r-1}

18.03.1951

M. wanted to give an example to show that one can really know sentences about physical objects. – If it were disputed whether one can have pain at the one or the other specific place of the body, then someone who is currently experiencing pain there could say: “I assure you, I have pain there now.” But it would sound strange if M. said: “I assure you, I know that this is a tree.” In this case, a personal experience is not of interest to us.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#66r.2) <span class="fac">[66r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/66r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-66r-2}

The important thing is only that it makes sense to say that one knows something; and then the assurance that one knows it cannot achieve anything here.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#66r.3+66v.1) <span class="fac">[66r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/66r.webp),[66v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/66v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-66r-3--66v-1}

Imagine a language-game: “If I call you, come in through the door.” In all ordinary cases, a doubt as to whether there really is a door will be impossible.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#66v.2+67r.1) <span class="fac">[66v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/66v.webp),[67r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-66v-2--67r-1}

What I must show is that a doubt is not necessary, even if it is possible. That the possibility of the language-game does not depend on the fact that everything that can be doubted is doubted. (This is connected with the role of contradiction in mathematics.)


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#67r.2) <span class="fac">[67r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-67r-2}

The sentence “I know that this is a tree” could, when said outside of its language-game, also be a quotation (from a German grammar, for example). – “But if I now _mean_ it, while I speak it?” The old misunderstanding concerning the word “mean.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#67r.3+67v.1) <span class="fac">[67r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67r.webp),[67v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-67r-3--67v-1}

“This is one of the things about which I cannot doubt.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#67v.2) <span class="fac">[67v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-67v-2}

“I know all of this.” And that will show itself in how I act and speak about things.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#67v.3) <span class="fac">[67v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-67v-3}

In the language-game “2,” can he say that he knows that these are building blocks? – “No, but he _knows_ it.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#67v.4+68r.1) <span class="fac">[67v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/67v.webp),[68r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-67v-4--68r-1}

Have I not made a mistake, and is not Moore completely right? Have I not made the elementary mistake of confusing what one thinks with what one knows? Of course, I do not think “The Earth existed some time before my birth,” but does that mean that I do not _know_ it? Do I not show that I know it by always drawing the consequences from it?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#68r.2) <span class="fac">[68r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-68r-2}

Do I not also know that no staircase from this house leads six stories deep into the earth, although I have never thought about it?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#68r.3+68v.1) <span class="fac">[68r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68r.webp),[68v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-68r-3--68v-1}

But does showing that I draw the consequences from it not only mean that I assume this hypothesis?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#68v.2) <span class="fac">[68v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-68v-2}

19.03.1951

Here I am inclined to fight against windmills, because I cannot yet say what I actually want to say.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#68v.3+69r.1) <span class="fac">[68v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/68v.webp),[69r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/69r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-68v-3--69r-1}

I want to say: Sentences of the form of statements of experience, and not only sentences of logic, belong to the foundation of all thinking (of language). – This statement is _not_ of the form “I know…” “I know…” expresses what _I_ know, and that is not of logical interest.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#69r.2) <span class="fac">[69r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/69r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-69r-2}

In this remark, the expression “sentences of the form of statements of experience” is already quite bad; it concerns statements about physical objects. And they do not serve as foundations, like hypotheses that, if they turn out to be false, are replaced by others.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#69v.1) <span class="fac">[69v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/69v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-69v-1}

… and feel free to write “In the beginning was the deed.”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#69v.2) <span class="fac">[69v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/69v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-69v-2}

To say, in Moore’s sense, that a human being _knows_ something; that what he says is therefore absolutely true, seems to me wrong. – It is true only insofar as it is an unwavering foundation of his language-games.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#69v.3+70r.1) <span class="fac">[69v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/69v.webp),[70r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/70r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-69v-3--70r-1}

I want to say: It is not the case that human beings, in certain respects, know the truth with absolute certainty. Rather, the absolute certainty relates only to their attitude.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#70r.2) <span class="fac">[70r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/70r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-70r-2}

But here, too, there is, of course, still a mistake.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#70r.3+70v.1) <span class="fac">[70r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/70r.webp),[70v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/70v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-70r-3--70v-1}

What I am aiming at also lies in the difference between the casual statement “I know that this is…,” as it is used in ordinary life, and this utterance when the philosopher makes it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#70v.2) <span class="fac">[70v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/70v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-70v-2}

For if M. says “I know that that … is”, I would like to respond: “You _know_ absolutely nothing!” – And yet, I would not make this response to the everyday use of these words. I therefore feel (rightly or wrongly?) that these two statements want to say something different.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#71r.1) <span class="fac">[71r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/71r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-71r-1}

If someone says that he _knows_ this & that, & this is part of his philosophy, then it is false if he has made a mistake in that statement.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#71r.2+71v.1) <span class="fac">[71r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/71r.webp),[71v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/71v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-71r-2--71v-1}

When I say “I know that is a foot” – what am I actually saying? Isn’t the whole point that I am certain of the consequences, that if someone else had doubted it, I could say to him, “See, I told you”? Would my knowledge still be worth anything if it failed as a guide to action? And _can_ it not fail?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#71v.2) <span class="fac">[71v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/71v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-71v-2}

20.03.1951

Our knowledge forms a large system. And only in this system does the individual have the value that we attribute to it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#71v.3+72r.1) <span class="fac">[71v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/71v.webp),[72r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/72r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-71v-3--72r-1}

When I say “_We assume_ that the Earth has existed for many years” (or something similar), it certainly sounds strange that we should _assume_ such a thing. But in the overall system of our language-games, it belongs to the foundation. One can say that the assumption forms the basis of action, & therefore naturally also of thinking.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#72r.2+72v.1) <span class="fac">[72r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/72r.webp),[72v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/72v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-72r-2--72v-1}

Anyone unable to imagine a situation in which one could say “I know that this is my hand” (and such situations are indeed rare) might say that these words are nonsense. However, he might also say: “Of course, I know it; how could I not know it?” – but in that case, he would perhaps understand the sentence “This is my hand” as an _explanation_ of the words “my hand”.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#72v.2+73r.1) <span class="fac">[72v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/72v.webp),[73r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/73r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-72v-2--73r-1}

For suppose you were guiding a blind man’s hand and saying, while guiding it along your own hand, “This is my hand”; if he then asked you, “Are you sure?” or “Do you know that?”, this would only make sense under very special circumstances.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#73r.2+73v.1) <span class="fac">[73r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/73r.webp),[73v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/73v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-73r-2--73v-1}

But on the other hand: How do I _know_ that this is my hand? Yes, do I even know exactly what it means to say that it is my hand? – When I say “How do I know it?”, I do not mean that I doubt it in the slightest. It is here a basis of all my actions. But it seems to me that it is wrongly expressed by the words “I know…”.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#73v.2+74r.1) <span class="fac">[73v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/73v.webp),[74r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/74r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-73v-2--74r-1}

Yes, isn't the use of the word “knowledge,” as a distinguished philosophical term, completely misguided? If “to know” has this significance, why not “to be certain”? Apparently, because it would be too subjective. But isn't “to know” just as subjective? Isn't one merely deluded by the grammatical peculiarity that “I know p” implies “p”? “I believe I know it” shouldn't express a lesser degree of certainty. – Yes, but one doesn't want to express subjective certainty, not even the greatest, but rather the fact that certain _sentences_ seem to underlie all questions & all thinking.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#74r.2+74v.1) <span class="fac">[74r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/74r.webp),[74v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/74v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-74r-2--74v-1}

Is a sentence such as, for example, that I have lived in this room for weeks, and that my memory does not deceive me in this, one of them?

– “certain beyond all reasonable doubt” –


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#74v.2+75r.1) <span class="fac">[74v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/74v.webp),[75r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/75r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-74v-2--75r-1}

21.03.1951

“I know that I have taken a bath every day in the last month.” What do I remember? Each day & the bath each morning? No. I _know_ that I have taken a bath every day & I do not derive this from some other immediate datum. Similarly, I say, “I felt a prick in my arm, without this location coming to my consciousness in some other way (through a picture, for example).”


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#75r.2) <span class="fac">[75r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/75r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-75r-2}

Is my understanding only blindness to my own misunderstanding? Often it seems that way to me.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#75r.3+75v.1) <span class="fac">[75r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/75r.webp),[75v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/75v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-75r-3--75v-1}

If I say “I have never been in Asia Minor,” where does this knowledge come from? I did not calculate it, no one told me; my memory tells me. – So can I not be mistaken about this? Is there a truth here that I _know_? – I cannot depart from this judgment without dragging all other judgments along with it.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#75v.2+76r.1+76v.1) <span class="fac">[75v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/75v.webp),[76r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/76r.webp),[76v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/76v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-75v-2--76r-1--76v-1}

Even a sentence such as the one stating that I am currently living in England has these two sides: it is not an _error_ – but on the other hand: what do I know about England? Could I not be completely mistaken in my judgments? Wouldn't it be possible for people to come into my room who would all say the opposite, yes, give me ‘proof’ for it, so that I would suddenly stand alone like a madman among normal people, or a normal person among the insane? Could not doubts arise in me about the simplest things?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#76v.2) <span class="fac">[76v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/76v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-76v-2}

I am in England – everything around me tells me so; as soon as I let my thoughts wander, & no matter where they go, they confirm it for me. – But could I not become mistaken if things happen that I cannot imagine now?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#76v.3+77r.1) <span class="fac">[76v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/76v.webp),[77r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/77r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-76v-3--77r-1}

So I want to say something that sounds like pragmatism. Here, a kind of world-view comes in my way.


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#77r.2+77v.1) <span class="fac">[77r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/77r.webp),[77v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/77v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-77r-2--77v-1}

Why then don't I simply say with M., “I _know_ that I am in England”? Saying this has _under certain circumstances_, which I can imagine, sense. But if I utter the sentence, not in these circumstances, to show that truths of this kind can be recognized by me with certainty, then it immediately becomes suspicious to me. – Is this justified?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#77v.2+78r.1) <span class="fac">[77v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/77v.webp),[78r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/78r.webp)</span> {#ms-175-77v-2--78r-1}

I say “I know p” either to assure that the truth p is also known to me, or simply as an intensification of ⊢p. One also says “I do not _believe_ it, I _know_ it.” And one could also express it like this (e.g.): “That is a tree. And that is not merely a conjecture.” But what about this: “If I were to communicate to someone that that is a tree, it would not be a mere conjecture.” Is this not what Moore wanted to say?


### [Ms-175](/ms-175/#78r.2+78v.1) <span class="fac">[78r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/78r.webp),[78v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-175/78v.webp)</span> {#ms-175-78r-2--78v-1}

It would not be a conjecture, & I could communicate it to the other with absolute certainty, as something that cannot be doubted. But does that mean that it is necessarily the truth? Can what I recognize with the greatest certainty as the tree that I have seen here all my life, can it not turn out to be something else? Can it not surprise me? And yet it was right, under the circumstances that give this sentence sense, to say “I know (I do not just conjecture) that that is a tree.” In truth, to say that I only believe it would be wrong. It would be completely _misleading_ to say: I believe that I am called L.W. And it is also true: I cannot be _wrong_ about that. But that does not mean that I am infallible in it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#22r.3+22v.1) <span class="fac">[22r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/22r.webp),[22v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/22v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-22r-3--22v-1}

21.03.1951

But how is one to _show_ that we do not only have knowledge of truths about sense data, but also about _things_? For it is not enough that someone assures us that _he_ knows this. What must one start from in order to show this?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#22v.2) <span class="fac">[22v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/22v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-22v-2}

22.03.1951

One must show that, even if he has never used the words “I know…”, his behaviour shows this, which is what matters to us.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#22v.3+23r.1) <span class="fac">[22v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/22v.webp),[23r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/23r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-22v-3--23r-1}

For how could one, if a normally acting person assured us: he only _believes_ that he is called so & so, he _believes_ he recognizes his constant housemates, he believes he has hands & feet, when he is not currently seeing them, etc. Can we show him from his actions (& speech) that it is not so?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#23r.2) <span class="fac">[23r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/23r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-23r-2}

March 23, 1951

What reason do I have now, since I cannot see my toes, to assume that I have five toes on each foot? Is it correct to say that the reason is that past experience has always taught me this? Am I more certain of past experience than I am of the fact that I have ten toes? That past experience may well be the _cause_ of my present certainty; but is it its ground?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#23r.3+23v.1) <span class="fac">[23r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/23r.webp),[23v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/23v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-23r-3--23v-1}

I meet a Martian & he asks me “How many toes do people have?” – I say: “Ten. I will show it to you” & take off my shoes. If he is now surprised that I knew this with such certainty, although I had not seen my toes. – Should I say: “We humans know that we have so many toes, whether we see them or not”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#23v.2+24r.1) <span class="fac">[23v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/23v.webp),[24r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/24r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-23v-2--24r-1}

26.03.1951

“I know that my room is on the second floor, that behind the door there is a short corridor leading to the stairs, etc.” One could imagine cases where I would make this utterance, but they would be rather rare. On the other hand, I demonstrate this knowledge daily through my actions & also in my speeches. What does the other person infer from these my actions & speeches? Not only that I am _certain_ about it? – From the fact that I have been living here for many weeks & have been going up & down the stairs daily, he will infer that I _know_ where my room is located. – I will use the statement “I know…” when he does _not_ yet know it, from which he would necessarily conclude that I possess this knowledge.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#24r.2) <span class="fac">[24r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/24r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-24r-2}

The statement “I know…” can only have its meaning in connection with the other evidence of ‘knowledge’.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#24r.3+24v.1) <span class="fac">[24r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/24r.webp),[24v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/24v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-24r-3--24v-1}

So, if I tell someone “I _know_ that that is a tree”, it is as if I were saying: “That is a tree; you can absolutely rely on it, there is no doubt.” And the philosopher could only use this to show that this form of speech is actually used. But if this is not merely to be a remark about German grammar, then he must specify the circumstances in which this expression functions.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#24v.2+25r.1) <span class="fac">[24v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/24v.webp),[25r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-24v-2--25r-1}

Does _experience_ now teach us that people, under these & those circumstances, know this & that?

Experience certainly shows us that, as a rule, a person is familiar with a house that they live in after so & so many days. Or, rather: experience teaches us that one can trust a person’s judgment after a certain period of instruction. According to experience, they must have learned for so & so long in order to make a correct prediction. But – – –.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25r.2) <span class="fac">[25r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25r-2}

27.03.1951

One is often bewitched by a word. E.g. by the word “know”.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25r.3) <span class="fac">[25r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25r-3}

Is God bound by our knowledge? _Can_ some of our statements not be false? For that is what we want to say.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25r.4+25v.1) <span class="fac">[25r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25r.webp),[25v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25r-4--25v-1}

I am inclined to say: “That _cannot_ be wrong”. That is interesting; but what are the implications?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25v.2) <span class="fac">[25v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25v-2}

It would not be enough to assure someone that I know what is happening here and there – without giving reasons that would convince the other person that I am capable of knowing it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25v.3) <span class="fac">[25v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25v-3}

Even the statement “I know that behind this door there is a corridor and the stairs down to the ground floor” sounds so convincing only because everyone knows that I know it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#25v.4) <span class="fac">[25v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/25v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-25v-4}

This is something general; it is not just something personal.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#26r.1) <span class="fac">[26r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/26r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-26r-1}

In the courtroom, the mere assertion of the witness “I know…” would not convince anyone. It must be shown that the witness was in a position to know. Even the assertion “I know that this is a hand,” while looking at one’s own hand, would not be credible if we did not know that he is looking at his own hand. And if we know it, it seems to assure that the speaker is normal in this respect.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#26r.2+26v.1) <span class="fac">[26r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/26r.webp),[26v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/26v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-26r-2--26v-1}

Could it not be the case that I am merely _imagining_ that I _know_ something?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#26v.2+27r.1) <span class="fac">[26v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/26v.webp),[27r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-26v-2--27r-1}

Suppose there is no word in a language that corresponds to our “know.” They simply state the assertion. “That is a tree,” etc. It can, of course, happen that they are mistaken. And now they add a sign to the sentence that indicates how likely they consider an error to be – or should I say: how likely an error is in this case? This latter can also be indicated by stating certain circumstances. E.g., “A said to B… I was standing very close to them, and my ears are good.” or “A was there yesterday. I saw him from a distance. My eyes are not very good.” or “There is a tree there. I see it clearly and have seen it countless times.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#27r.2) <span class="fac">[27r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-27r-2}

“The train leaves at 2 o’clock. Check again to be sure.” or “The train leaves at 2 o’clock. I just checked in a new timetable.” One can also add “I am reliable in such matters.” The usefulness of such additions is obvious.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#27r.3+27v.1) <span class="fac">[27r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27r.webp),[27v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-27r-3--27v-1}

But if I say “I have two hands” – what can I add to indicate reliability? At most, that the circumstances are normal.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#27v.2) <span class="fac">[27v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-27v-2}

Why am I so certain that this is my hand? Isn’t this entire language-game based on this kind of certainty? Or: Isn’t this ‘certainty’ already presupposed in the language-game? Namely, by the fact that _he_ doesn’t play it, or plays it incorrectly, by not recognizing the objects.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#27v.3+28r.1) <span class="fac">[27v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/27v.webp),[28r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/28r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-27v-3--28r-1}

March 28, 1951

Compare this with 12 × 12 = 144. Here, too, we do not say “perhaps.” For as long as this sentence is based on the fact that we do not miscount or miscalculate, that our senses do not deceive us when calculating, the two sentences, the arithmetic and the physical, are on the same level. I mean: the arithmetic game is just as little uncertain as the physical one. But this can be misunderstood. My remark is a logical one, not a psychological one.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#28r.2+28v.1) <span class="fac">[28r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/28r.webp),[28v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/28v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-28r-2--28v-1}

I mean: if one is not surprised that arithmetic sentences (e.g., the multiplication table) are ‘absolutely certain,’ why should one be surprised that the sentence “This is my hand” is equally so?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#28v.2) <span class="fac">[28v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/28v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-28v-2}

Something must be taught to us as a basis.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#28v.3+29r.1) <span class="fac">[28v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/28v.webp),[29r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-28v-3--29r-1}

I want to say: Our learning takes the form “This is a violet,” “This is a table.” The child, however, might hear the word “violet” for the first time in the sentence “This is perhaps a violet”; then it might ask, “What is a violet?” Now, of course, this could be answered by showing it a _picture_. But what if, while showing a picture, one only said, “This is a…,” but otherwise always said, “This is perhaps a…?” – What practical consequences should this have? A doubt that doubted everything would be no doubt.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#29r.2) <span class="fac">[29r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-29r-2}

My objection to Moore, that the sense of the isolated sentence “That is a tree” is indeterminate, because it is not determined what the ‘_That_’ is, of which one asserts that it is a tree, – does not hold; for one can make the sense more determinate by, for example, saying: “The object there, which looks like a tree, is not an artificial imitation of a tree, but a real tree.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#29r.3+29v.1) <span class="fac">[29r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29r.webp),[29v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-29r-3--29v-1}

It wouldn’t be reasonable to doubt whether that is a real tree or… The fact that it seems obvious to me is not the point. If it would be unreasonable to doubt here, then this cannot be inferred from my point of view. There would therefore have to be a rule that declares doubt unreasonable here. But there isn’t one either.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#29v.2) <span class="fac">[29v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-29v-2}

I do, however, say: “No reasonable person would doubt this.” Could one imagine that learned judges would be asked whether a doubt is reasonable or unreasonable?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#29v.3+30r.1) <span class="fac">[29v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/29v.webp),[30r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-29v-3--30r-1}

There are cases in which doubt is unreasonable, but others in which it seems logically impossible. And there doesn’t seem to be a clear boundary between them.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#30r.2) <span class="fac">[30r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-30r-2}

29.03.1951

The language-game is based on the fact that words & objects are recognized. We learn with the same certainty that this is an armchair as we learn that 2 × 2 = 4.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#30r.3) <span class="fac">[30r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-30r-3}

So if I doubt, or am uncertain, whether that is my hand (in whatever sense), why not also about the meaning of these words?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#30r.4+30v.1) <span class="fac">[30r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30r.webp),[30v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-30r-4--30v-1}

Am I saying that certainty lies in the essence of the language-game?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#30v.2) <span class="fac">[30v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-30v-2}

One doubts for specific reasons. The question is: How is doubt introduced into the language-game?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#30v.3) <span class="fac">[30v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/30v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-30v-3}

If the merchant wanted to examine each of his apples without reason, just to be absolutely sure, why doesn’t he then examine the examination? And can one now speak of belief here (I mean, in the sense of religious belief, not of conjecture)? All psychological words only distract from the main point.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#31r.1+31v.1) <span class="fac">[31r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/31r.webp),[31v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/31v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-31r-1--31v-1}

I go to the doctor, show him my hand & say “This is a hand, not…; I injured it, etc. etc.” Am I just making an unnecessary communication? Couldn’t one, for example, say: Suppose the words “This is my hand” are a communication – how could you then be sure that he understands the communication? Yes, if there is a doubt about ‘that being a hand’, why isn’t there also a doubt about me being a person who communicates this to the doctor? – On the other hand, one can imagine – although very strange – cases in which such an explanation is not superfluous, or is only superfluous but not absurd.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#31v.2+32r.1) <span class="fac">[31v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/31v.webp),[32r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/32r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-31v-2--32r-1}

Suppose I were the doctor & a patient comes to me, shows me his hand & says: “What looks like a hand here is not an excellent imitation, but a real hand.” (After which he talks about his injury.) – Would I really see this as a communication, even if it is superfluous? Wouldn’t I rather consider it nonsense, which, however, has the form of a communication? For, I would say, if this communication really had sense, how can he be sure of his case? The communication lacks context.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#32r.2) <span class="fac">[32r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/32r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-32r-2}

March 30, 1951

Why does Moore, when listing the things he knows, not mention, for example, that there is a village in a certain part of England called so-and-so? In other words, why does he not mention a fact that is known to him, & not to _everyone_ of us?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#32r.3) <span class="fac">[32r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/32r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-32r-3}

31.03.1951

It is certain that the communication “This is a tree”, if no one could doubt it, could be a kind of joke & have sense as such. Renan actually made such a joke once.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#32v.1) <span class="fac">[32v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/32v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-32v-1}

03.04.1951

My difficulty can also be demonstrated in this way: I am sitting with a friend in conversation. Suddenly I say: “I have known all along that you are N.N.” Is this really just a superfluous, albeit true, remark? It seems to me as if these words are similar to a “hello”, if one said it to the other person in the middle of a conversation.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#32v.2+33r.1) <span class="fac">[32v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/32v.webp),[33r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/33r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-32v-2--33r-1}

How about the words “It is known today that there are over … species of insects”, instead of the words “I know that this is a tree”? If someone suddenly said that sentence out of context, one could think that he had been thinking about something else in the meantime & is now saying a sentence from his train of thought. Or also: he is in a trance & speaks without understanding his words.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#33r.2) <span class="fac">[33r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/33r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-33r-2}

So it seems to me that I have known something all along, and yet it doesn’t make sense to say it, to utter this truth!


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#33r.3+33v.1) <span class="fac">[33r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/33r.webp),[33v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/33v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-33r-3--33v-1}

I am sitting with a philosopher in the garden; he repeatedly says, “I know that that is a tree,” while pointing to a tree nearby. A third person comes along and hears this, and I say to him, “This person is not crazy. We are just philosophizing.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#33v.2+34r.1) <span class="fac">[33v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/33v.webp),[34r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-33v-2--34r-1}

04.04.1951

Someone says irrelevantly, “That is a tree.” He might say the sentence because he remembers having heard it in a similar situation; or he was suddenly struck by the beauty of this tree and the sentence was an exclamation; or he said the sentence to himself as a grammatical example. (Etc.) Now I ask him: “What did you mean by that?” and he answers: “It was a communication directed at you.” Wouldn’t it be up to me to assume that he doesn’t know what he is saying, if he is crazy enough to want to make this communication to me?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34r.2) <span class="fac">[34r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34r-2}

Someone says to me in conversation, unconnectedly, “I wish you all the best.” I am surprised; but later I realize that these words are connected with his thoughts about me. And now they no longer seem senseless to me.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34r.3) <span class="fac">[34r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34r-3}

Why is there no doubt that my name is L.W.? It seems to be nothing that one could definitively establish without further question. One shouldn’t think that this is one of the undeniable truths.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34r.4+34v.1) <span class="fac">[34r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34r.webp),[34v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34r-4--34v-1}

05.04.1951

[Here there is still a large gap in my thinking. And I doubt whether it will ever be filled.]


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34v.2) <span class="fac">[34v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34v-2}

It is so difficult to find the _beginning_. Or rather: it is difficult to begin at the beginning. And not to try to go further back.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34v.3) <span class="fac">[34v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34v-3}

When the child learns the language, it learns at the same time what to investigate, and what not to investigate. When it learns that there is a cupboard in the room, one does not teach it to doubt whether what it later sees is still a cupboard, or only a kind of backdrop.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#34v.4+35r.1) <span class="fac">[34v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/34v.webp),[35r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/35r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-34v-4--35r-1}

Just as one learns a certain basic form when writing and then later varies it, so one first learns the constancy of things as the norm, which is then subject to change.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#35r.2) <span class="fac">[35r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/35r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-35r-2}

This game proves itself. That may be the reason why it is played, but it is not the reason.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#35r.3) <span class="fac">[35r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/35r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-35r-3}

I want to consider the human being here as an animal. As a being in a primitive state. Because whatever logic is sufficient for a primitive means of communication, we need not be ashamed of it. Language did not arise from a line of reasoning.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#35v.1) <span class="fac">[35v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/35v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-35v-1}

06.04.1951

The child does not learn that there are books, that there are armchairs, etc., etc., but it learns to fetch books, to sit (on) armchairs, etc. Of course, questions about existence also arise later: “Is there a unicorn?” etc. But such a question is only possible because, as a rule, no such question arises. Because how does one know how to convince oneself of the existence of the unicorn? How did one learn the method of determining whether something exists or not?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#35v.2+36r.1) <span class="fac">[35v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/35v.webp),[36r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-35v-2--36r-1}

“So one must know that the objects exist whose names one teaches a child by means of an ostensive explanation.” – Why must one know it? Isn’t it enough that experience later does not prove the contrary? Why should the language-game rest on knowledge?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#36r.2) <span class="fac">[36r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-36r-2}

07.04.1951

Does the child believe that there is milk? Or does it know that there is milk? Does the cat know that there is a mouse?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#36r.3) <span class="fac">[36r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-36r-3}

Should we say that the knowledge that there are physical objects is a very early one, or a very late one?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#36r.4+36v.1) <span class="fac">[36r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36r.webp),[36v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-36r-4--36v-1}

08.04.1951

The child that learns to use the word “tree.” One stands with it in front of a tree & says “_Beautiful_ tree!” That there is no doubt about the existence of the tree entering the language-game is clear. But can one say that the child _knows_: that there is a tree? It is certainly true that ‘to know something’ does not in itself include: to _think_ about it – but must not someone who knows something be capable of doubt? And to doubt means to think.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#36v.2+37r.1) <span class="fac">[36v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/36v.webp),[37r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-36v-2--37r-1}

When one hears M. say “I _know_ that that is a tree,” one suddenly understands those who think that is not at all established. The matter suddenly seems unclear & blurred to one. It is as if M. has shone the wrong light on it. It is as if I see a painting (perhaps a stage set) & immediately recognize from a distance, without the slightest doubt, what it depicts. But now I approach, & then I see a lot of spots of different colors, all of which are highly ambiguous & give no certainty at all.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#37r.2) <span class="fac">[37r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-37r-2}

It is as if the “I know” cannot stand any metaphysical emphasis.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#37r.3+37v.1) <span class="fac">[37r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37r.webp),[37v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-37r-3--37v-1}

Correct use of the word “I know.” A person with poor eyesight asks me: “Do you think that what we see there is a tree?” – I reply, “I _know_ it; I see it clearly & know it well.” – A: “Is N.N. at home?” – I: “I think so.” – A: “Was he at home yesterday?” – I: “He was at home yesterday; I know that; I spoke to him.” –

A: “Do you know, or do you only believe, that this part of the house was newly built?” – I: “I _know_ it; I inquired with the…”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#37v.2) <span class="fac">[37v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-37v-2}

Here, one says “I know” & gives the reason for the knowledge, or one can give it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#37v.3+38r.1) <span class="fac">[37v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/37v.webp),[38r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-37v-3--38r-1}

One can also imagine a case in which someone goes through a list of sentences & repeatedly asks himself, “Do I know this, or do I only believe it?” He wants to check the certainty of each individual sentence. It could be a witness statement in court.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#38r.2) <span class="fac">[38r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-38r-2}

09.04.1951

“Do you know, or do you only believe, that you are called L.W.?” Is that a meaningful question? Do you know, or do you only believe, that what you write here are German words? Do you only believe that “believe” has _this_ meaning? _Which_ meaning?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#38r.3) <span class="fac">[38r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-38r-3}

What is the proof that I _know_ something? Surely it is not that I say that I know it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#38r.4+38v.1) <span class="fac">[38r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38r.webp),[38v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-38r-4--38v-1}

So, if authors list all the things they _know_, that proves nothing. That one can know something about physical things cannot be proven by the assertions of those who believe they know it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#38v.2) <span class="fac">[38v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-38v-2}

For what does one answer to the one who says, “I think it only seems to you that you know it”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#38v.3+39r.1) <span class="fac">[38v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/38v.webp),[39r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-38v-3--39r-1}

If now I ask, “Do I know, or do I only believe, that I am called…” then it is of no use to look into myself. But I could say: Not only do I never doubt that I am called that, but I could not be certain of any judgment if doubt arose about it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#39r.2) <span class="fac">[39r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-39r-2}

10.04.1951

“Do I know, or do I only believe, that I am called L.W.?” – If the question were “Am I certain, or do I only suspect, that I am…?”, then I could rely on my answer.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#39r.3+39v.1) <span class="fac">[39r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39r.webp),[39v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-39r-3--39v-1}

“Do I know, or do I only believe …?” one could also express it this way: How, if it were to turn out that what previously seemed inaccessible to _doubt_ is false? Would I react as if a belief had proven false, or would that seem to undermine the very basis of my judgment? – But of course, I do not want to make a _prediction_ here. Would I simply say “I never would have thought that!” – or would I have to refuse to revise my judgment because such a ‘revision’ would be equivalent to the annihilation of all standards?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#39v.2) <span class="fac">[39v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-39v-2}

Is it therefore the case that I must recognize certain authorities in order to be able to judge at all?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#39v.3) <span class="fac">[39v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/39v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-39v-3}

“I cannot doubt this sentence without giving up all judgment.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#40r.1) <span class="fac">[40r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-40r-1}

But what kind of sentence is that?

(It reminds one of what Frege said about the law of identity.) It is certainly not an empirical proposition. It does not belong to psychology. It has more the character of a rule.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#40r.2) <span class="fac">[40r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-40r-2}

One could simply say to someone who wants to raise objections to certain _certain_ sentences, “Oh, nonsense!” That is, not answer him, but reprimand him.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#40r.3) <span class="fac">[40r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-40r-3}

Here, it is a similar case to when one shows that it makes no sense to say that a game has always been played incorrectly.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#40r.4+40v.1) <span class="fac">[40r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40r.webp),[40v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-40r-4--40v-1}

If someone wanted to raise doubt in me & said: here your memory is deceiving you, there you were cheated, there again you didn’t convince yourself thoroughly enough, etc., & I didn’t allow myself to be shaken & remained with my certainty – then that could not be wrong for that reason alone, because it (only) defines a game.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#40v.2) <span class="fac">[40v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/40v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-40v-2}

11.04.1951

The strange thing is that, even if I think it is perfectly correct that one rejects the attempt to make one doubt in the fundamentals with the word “Nonsense!”, I think it is wrong if he wants to defend himself, using words like “I know.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#41r.1) <span class="fac">[41r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-41r-1}

I could also say this: The ‘law of induction’ can no more be _justified_ than certain particular sentences concerning the empirical material.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#41r.2) <span class="fac">[41r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-41r-2}

But it also seems to me to be nonsense to say “I know that the law of induction is true.” Imagine such a statement made in a courtroom! It would be more correct to say “I believe in the law…”, where ‘believe’ has nothing to do with _guessing_.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#41r.3+41v.1) <span class="fac">[41r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41r.webp),[41v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-41r-3--41v-1}

Am I not increasingly coming to the conclusion that logic cannot be described in the end? You must look at the practice of language, then you will see it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#41v.2) <span class="fac">[41v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-41v-2}

Could one say “I know the location of my hands with my eyes closed,” if my statement always or mostly contradicted the testimony of others?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#41v.3+42r.1) <span class="fac">[41v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/41v.webp),[42r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-41v-3--42r-1}

I look at an object & say “That is a tree” or “I know that it…” – Now, if I go closer & it turns out to be different, then I can say “It wasn’t a tree after all”; or I say “It _was_ a tree, but it isn’t one now.” If, however, everyone else disagreed with me & said that it had never been a tree, & if all other testimony was against me – what _use_ would it be to insist on my “I know…”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42r.2) <span class="fac">[42r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42r-2}

Whether I _know_ something depends on whether the evidence supports me or contradicts me. Because to say that one knows that one is in pain means nothing.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42r.3) <span class="fac">[42r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42r-3}

It is always by the grace of nature when one knows something.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42r.4+42v.1) <span class="fac">[42r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42r.webp),[42v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42r-4--42v-1}

If my memory _here_ deceives me, then it can deceive me everywhere. If I don’t know _that_, how do I know whether my words mean what I believe them to mean?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42v.2) <span class="fac">[42v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42v-2}

“If this deceives me, what does ‘deceive’ mean then?”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42v.3) <span class="fac">[42v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42v-3}

What can I rely on?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42v.4) <span class="fac">[42v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42v-4}

I actually want to say that a language-game is only possible if one relies on something. (I didn’t say “can rely on something.”)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#42v.5+43r.1) <span class="fac">[42v\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/42v.webp),[43r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-42v-5--43r-1}

If I say “I _know_ that this is a towel,” then I am making an _utterance_. I am not thinking about a verification. For me, it is an immediate utterance. I am not thinking about the past or the future. (And that is, of course, also the case with Moore.) Just like an immediate reaching; like I reach for the towel without doubting.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#43r.2) <span class="fac">[43r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-43r-2}

But this immediate reaching does correspond to a _certainty_, not knowledge. But doesn’t this also involve reaching for the name of a thing?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#43r.3+43v.1) <span class="fac">[43r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43r.webp),[43v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-43r-3--43v-1}

12.04.1951

The question is: “How, if you had to change your view even in these most fundamental things?” And the answer seems to me to be: “You _don’t_ have to change them. Precisely in that lies the fact that they are ‘fundamental.’”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#43v.2) <span class="fac">[43v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-43v-2}

What if something _really unheard of_ happened? For example, if I saw houses gradually turning into steam without any obvious cause; if the animals in the meadow stood on their heads, laughed, and spoke understandable words; if trees gradually turned into people, and people into trees. Was I right when I said before all these events, “I know that this is a house,” etc., or simply “This is a house,” etc.?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#43v.3+44r.1) <span class="fac">[43v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/43v.webp),[44r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-43v-3--44r-1}

This statement seemed fundamental to me; if that is wrong, what is still ‘true’ and ‘false’?!


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#44r.2) <span class="fac">[44r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-44r-2}

If my name is _not_ L.W., how can I rely on what is meant by “true” and “false”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#44r.3) <span class="fac">[44r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-44r-3}

If something happened (if, for example, someone said something to me) that would be likely to raise doubts in me, then there would certainly also be something that would make the reasons for such doubts themselves doubtful, and I could therefore decide to stick to my old belief.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#44v.1) <span class="fac">[44v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-44v-1}

Wouldn’t it be possible for something to happen that would completely throw me off track? Evidence that would make the most certain thing seem unacceptable to me? Or would it at least cause me to overturn my most fundamental judgments? (Whether rightly or wrongly is irrelevant here.)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#44v.2) <span class="fac">[44v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-44v-2}

Could I imagine observing this in another person?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#44v.3+45r.1+45v.1) <span class="fac">[44v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/44v.webp),[45r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/45r.webp),[45v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/45v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-44v-3--45r-1--45v-1}

If you follow the command, “Bring me a book,” then it is certainly possible that you must examine whether what you see there is actually a book, but you do know what is meant by “book”; and if you don’t know, then you can look it up – but then you must know what another word means. And that a word means this and that, that it is used in this way, is a fact of the same kind as the fact that what you see there is a book. In order to be able to follow a command, you must be certain about an empirical fact. Yes, doubt is based only on what is beyond doubt. But since a language-game is something that consists of repeated game-actions in time, it seems that one cannot say in any _individual_ case that this and that must be beyond doubt if there is to be a language-game, but rather that, _as a rule_, some empirical judgments must be beyond doubt.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#45v.2+46r.1) <span class="fac">[45v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/45v.webp),[46r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/46r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-45v-2--46r-1}

13.04.1951

Moore is perfectly justified in saying that he knows there is a tree in front of him. Of course, he could be mistaken. (Because this is not like the statement “I believe there is a tree there.”) But whether he is right in this case or mistaken is not philosophically relevant. If M. is arguing against those who say that one cannot really know something like that, then he cannot do so by asserting: _he_ knows this and that. Because one does not have to believe him for that. If his opponents had claimed that one could not _believe_ this and that, then he could have replied to them, “_I_ believe it.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#46r.2) <span class="fac">[46r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/46r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-46r-2}

14.04.1951

Moore’s mistake lies in responding to the claim that one cannot know something by saying, “I know it.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#46r.3+46v.1) <span class="fac">[46r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/46r.webp),[46v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/46v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-46r-3--46v-1}

We say: if the child masters the language – and therefore its application – he must know the meanings of the words. He must, for example, be able to assign the color name to white, black, red, blue things without any doubt.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#46v.2) <span class="fac">[46v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/46v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-46v-2}

Yes, here no one is missing the doubt; no one is surprised that we do not just _assume_ the meaning of the words.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#51v.2+52r.1+52v.1+53r.1) <span class="fac">[51v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/51v.webp),[52r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/52r.webp),[52v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/52v.webp),[53r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-51v-2--52r-1--52v-1--53r-1}

Is it essential for our language-games (e.g., ‘commanding & obeying’) that doubt does not arise at certain points, or is it enough if there is a feeling of certainty, even if tinged with a slight doubt? Is it enough if I don't, as now, _without further ado_, without the interruption of any doubt, call something ‘black’, ‘green’, ‘red’ – but instead say “I am certain that it is red”, as one might say “I am certain that he will come today” (i.e., with the ‘feeling of certainty’)? The accompanying feeling is, of course, irrelevant to us, and we also have no need to worry about the words “I am certain that”. – What is important is whether a difference in the _practice_ of language is associated with this. One might ask whether it occurs in all cases where we, for example, assert something with certainty (in an experiment, for example, we look into a tube and report the color we observe through it), whether it says “I am certain” on this occasion. If it does, one would first be inclined to check its statement. But if it turns out that it is quite reliable, one would explain that its way of speaking is merely an idiosyncrasy that does not affect the matter. One might, for example, assume that it has read skeptical philosophers, has been convinced that one cannot know anything, and therefore has adopted this way of speaking. Once we are used to it, it makes no difference to the practice.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#53r.2) <span class="fac">[53r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-53r-2}

What does it look like when someone has a different relationship to color names, for example, than we do? Where, namely, a slight doubt, or the possibility of doubt, exists in its use.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#53r.3+53v.1) <span class="fac">[53r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53r.webp),[53v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-53r-3--53v-1}

16.04.1951

If someone, when looking at an English postbox, said: “I am certain that it is red”, we would have to consider him colorblind, or believe that he does not master German and knows the correct color name in another language. If neither of these were the case, we would not understand him properly.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#53v.2) <span class="fac">[53v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-53v-2}

A German who calls this color “red” is not: ‘certain that it is called “red” in German’. The child who masters the use of the word is not ‘certain that this color is called _that_ in its language’. One cannot say that it learns, when it learns to speak, that the color is called that in German; or that it _knows_ this when it has learned the use of the word.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#53v.3+54r.1) <span class="fac">[53v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/53v.webp),[54r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-53v-3--54r-1}

And yet: if someone asked me how the color is called in German, and I tell him, and he asks me “Are you certain?”: I will answer: “I _know_ it; German is my mother tongue”.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#54r.2) <span class="fac">[54r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-54r-2}

A child will also say, or say of itself, that it already knows how this and that is called.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#54r.3) <span class="fac">[54r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-54r-3}

I can say to someone “This color is called ‘red’ in German” (if I am, for example, teaching him German). In this case, I would not say “I know that this color…” – I would say that if I had just learned it myself, or in contrast to another color whose German name I do not know.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#54v.1) <span class="fac">[54v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-54v-1}

But is it not correct to describe my present state _thus_: I _know_ how this color is called in German? And if that is correct, why should I not describe my state with the corresponding words “I know etc.”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#54v.2) <span class="fac">[54v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-54v-2}

Moore, then, when, sitting in front of the tree, he said “I know that this is a…”, simply expressed the truth about his state at that time.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#54v.3+55r.1) <span class="fac">[54v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/54v.webp),[55r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-54v-3--55r-1}

[I am now philosophizing like an old woman who keeps misplacing something and has to look for it again; sometimes it’s her glasses, sometimes her keyring –]


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#55r.2) <span class="fac">[55r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-55r-2}

Well, if it was correct to describe his state outside of the context, then it was also correct to utter the words “That is a tree” outside of the context.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#55r.3+55v.1) <span class="fac">[55r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55r.webp),[55v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-55r-3--55v-1}

But is it wrong to say: “The child who masters a language-game must _know_ certain things”? If, instead, one said “must _be able_ to do certain things”, that would be a pleonasm, and yet it is precisely _that_ to which I would like to reply to the first sentence. – But: “The child acquires natural history knowledge.” This presupposes that the child can ask what the plant is called.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#55v.3) <span class="fac">[55v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-55v-3}

The child knows how something is called when it can answer the question “What is this called?” correctly.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#55v.4+56r.1) <span class="fac">[55v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/55v.webp),[56r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-55v-4--56r-1}

The child, which is beginning to learn the language, naturally does not yet have the concept of _being called_.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56r.2) <span class="fac">[56r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56r-2}

Can one say of someone who does not have this concept that he _knows_ how this and that are called?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56r.3) <span class="fac">[56r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56r-3}

The child, I would like to say, learns to react in such and such a way; & when it does so, it still does not know anything. Knowledge only begins at a later stage.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56r.4) <span class="fac">[56r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56r-4}

Is it with knowledge as it is with collecting?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56v.1) <span class="fac">[56v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56v-1}

A dog could learn to run to N when it hears the call “N” & to M when it hears the call “M,” – but would it therefore know the names of people?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56v.2) <span class="fac">[56v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56v-2}

“He first knows how this is called, but not yet how that is called.” Strictly speaking, one cannot say this of someone who does not yet have the concept that people have names.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56v.3) <span class="fac">[56v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56v-3}

“I cannot describe this flower if I do not know that this color is called ‘red.’”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#56v.4+57r.1) <span class="fac">[56v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/56v.webp),[57r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-56v-4--57r-1}

The child can use the names of people long before it can say in any form: “I know how this one is called; I do not yet know how that one is called.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57r.2) <span class="fac">[57r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57r-2}

I can, of course, truthfully say “I know what this color is called in German,” by, for example, pointing to the color of fresh blood. But – – –


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57r.3) <span class="fac">[57r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57r-3}

17.04.1951

‘The child knows what color the word “blue” means.’ What it knows here is not so simple.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57r.4) <span class="fac">[57r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57r-4}

“I know what this color is called,” I would say, for example, if it is a matter of shades of color whose names not everyone knows.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57r.5+57v.1) <span class="fac">[57r\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57r.webp),[57v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57r-5--57v-1}

One cannot say to a child who is just beginning to speak & can use the words “red” & “blue”: “Isn’t it true that you know what this color is called?”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57v.2) <span class="fac">[57v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57v-2}

The child must learn the use of color names before it can ask for the name of a color.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#57v.3) <span class="fac">[57v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/57v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-57v-3}

It would be wrong to say that I can only say “I know that there is an armchair there” if there is an armchair there. Of course, it is only then _true_, but I have a right to say it if I am _certain_ that there is one there, even if I am wrong.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#58r.1) <span class="fac">[58r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-58r-1}

18.04.1951

The pretensions are a mortgage that burdens the philosopher’s power of thought.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#58r.2) <span class="fac">[58r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-58r-2}

If someone believes something, one does not always have to be able to answer the question ‘why he believes it’; but if he knows something, the question “How does he know it?” must be answerable.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#58r.3) <span class="fac">[58r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-58r-3}

And if one answers this question, it must be done according to generally accepted principles. _Thus_ one knows such and such.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#58r.4+58v.1) <span class="fac">[58r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58r.webp),[58v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-58r-4--58v-1}

Do I know that I am sitting in an armchair right now? — Don’t I know it?! Under the present circumstances, no one would say that I know; but no more, for example, that I am conscious. One would not usually say this of passers-by in the street either. But if one doesn’t say it, does that mean it _is_ not the case??


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#58v.2+59r.1) <span class="fac">[58v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/58v.webp),[59r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/59r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-58v-2--59r-1}

It is strange: If, without any particular reason, I say “I know,” e.g., “I know that I am now sitting in an armchair,” the sentence seems unjustified & presumptuous to me. But if I make the same statement where there is a need for it, it seems to me, although I am not a hair’s breadth more certain of its truth, as perfectly justified & commonplace.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#59r.2) <span class="fac">[59r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/59r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-59r-2}

In its language-game, it is not presumptuous. It does not stand higher than just the human language-game: For it has its (very) special application. But the way I say the sentence outside of its context, it appears in a false light. For then it is as if I wanted to assert that there are things that I _know_. About which God himself could not tell me anything.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#59v.1+60r.1) <span class="fac">[59v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/59v.webp),[60r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-59v-1--60r-1}

19.04.1951

We say, we know that water boils when it is put on the fire. How do we know it? Experience has taught us that. – I say “I know that I had breakfast this morning,” experience has not taught me that. One also says “I know that he is in pain.” Each time, the language-game is different, each time we are _certain_, & each time one will agree with us that we _are capable_ of knowing. That is why the principles of physics can also be found in textbooks for everyone. If someone says that he _knows_ something, then it must be something that, according to general opinion, he is capable of knowing.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#60r.2) <span class="fac">[60r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-60r-2}

One does not say: He is capable of believing that. But one does say: “It is reasonable in this situation to assume that” (or “to believe it”).


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#60r.3) <span class="fac">[60r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-60r-3}

A military court may have to judge whether it was reasonable in this situation to assume that & that with certainty (even if falsely).


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#60r.4+60v.1) <span class="fac">[60r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60r.webp),[60v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-60r-4--60v-1}

We say, we know that water boils under such & such circumstances & does not freeze. Is it conceivable that we are mistaken in this? Wouldn’t a mistake invalidate all judgment? I mean: What could remain valid if that were to fall? Could anyone find something, & we now say: “It was a mistake”? Whatever may happen in the future, however water may behave in the future, – we _know_ that until now it has behaved in countless cases _in this way_. This fact is embedded in the foundations of our language-game.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#60v.2+61r.1) <span class="fac">[60v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/60v.webp),[61r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-60v-2--61r-1}
