You must bear in mind that the language-game is, in a way, something unpredictable. I mean: It is not justified. Not reasonable (or unreasonable). It stands there – like our life.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#61r.2) <span class="fac">[61r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-61r-2}

And the concept of knowledge is linked to that of the language-game.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#61r.3) <span class="fac">[61r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-61r-3}

“I know” & “You can rely on it.” But one cannot always substitute the first for the second.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#61r.4) <span class="fac">[61r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-61r-4}

At any rate, it is important to imagine a language in which there is no _our_ concept of ‘knowing’.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#61r.5+61v.1+62r.1) <span class="fac">[61r\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61r.webp),[61v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/61v.webp),[62r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/62r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-61r-5--61v-1--62r-1}

One says “I _know_ that he is in pain,” even though one cannot give a convincing reason for it. – Is that the same as “I am certain that he is…”? – No. “I am certain” gives you the subjective certainty. “I know” means that there is a difference of understanding between me, who knows it, & the one who does not know it. (Roughly based on a difference in the degree of experience.) If I say in mathematics “I know,” then the justification for it is a proof. If, in these two cases, one says instead of “I know,” “You can rely on it,” then the justification is of a different kind each time. And the justification has an end.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#62r.2) <span class="fac">[62r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/62r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-62r-2}

A language-game: Bringing the building blocks, stating the number of blocks present. Sometimes the number is estimated, sometimes it is determined by counting. The question then arises: “Do you think there are that many blocks?” & the answer: “I know it, I just counted them.” But here the “I know” could be omitted. However, if there are several ways of reliably ascertaining it, such as counting, weighing, measuring the pile, etc., then instead of stating _how_ one knows it, the statement “I know” can be used.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#62v.1) <span class="fac">[62v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/62v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-62v-1}

But here we are not yet talking about a ‘knowledge’ that _this_ “plate,” _this_ “pillar,” etc., is called.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#62v.2+63r.1) <span class="fac">[62v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/62v.webp),[63r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-62v-2--63r-1}

Yes, the child who learns my language-game (No. 2) does not learn to say “I know that this is called ‘plate’.” Of course, there is now a language-game in which the child uses _this_ sentence. This presupposes that the child, once it has been given the name, can already use it. As if someone said to me, “This color is called ‘…’” – So if the child has learned a language-game with building blocks, then one can now say, for example, “And _this_ stone is called ‘…’,” & this has thereby _extended_ the original language-game.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63r.2) <span class="fac">[63r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63r-2}

And is the knowledge that I am called L. W. of the same kind as that water boils at 100˚ C? This question is, of course, wrongly formulated.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63r.3) <span class="fac">[63r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63r-3}

If one of my names were only used very rarely, then it might be that I do not know it. The fact that I know my name is self-evident only because I, like everyone else, use it countless times.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63r.4+63v.1) <span class="fac">[63r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63r.webp),[63v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63r-4--63v-1}

An inner experience cannot show me that I _know_ something. If, therefore, I still say “I know that I am called…” and it is clearly not an experiential proposition – – –


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63v.2) <span class="fac">[63v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63v-2}

“I know that I am called so; everyone knows their name among us adults.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63v.3) <span class="fac">[63v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63v-3}

“I am called…, you can rely on that. If it turns out to be false, then you will never have to believe me again.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#63v.4+64r.1) <span class="fac">[63v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/63v.webp),[64r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-63v-4--64r-1}

I seem to know that I cannot be mistaken about my own name! This is expressed by: “If that is false, then I am crazy.” Well, but these are just words; but what influence does it have on the application of language?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#64r.2) <span class="fac">[64r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-64r-2}

Because I cannot be convinced of the opposite by anything?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#64r.3) <span class="fac">[64r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-64r-3}

The question is: What _kind_ of sentence is this: “I know that I cannot be mistaken in this,” or also: “I cannot be mistaken in this”? The “I know” seems to cut off all reasons here: I simply _know_ it. But if there can be talk of error here at all, then it must be possible to examine whether I know it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#64v.1) <span class="fac">[64v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-64v-1}

The word “I know” could therefore have the purpose of indicating where I am reliable, whereby the usefulness of this sign must emerge from _experience_.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#64v.2) <span class="fac">[64v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-64v-2}

One could ask, “How do I know that I am not mistaken about my name?” – & if the answer were “Because I have used it so often,” one could ask further: “How do I know that I am not mistaken _in that_?!” And here the “How do I know” can no longer have any meaning.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#64v.3+65r.1) <span class="fac">[64v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/64v.webp),[65r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-64v-3--65r-1}

“I know my name with complete certainty.” I would refuse to consider any argument that wanted to show the opposite! And what does it mean: “I _would_ refuse”? Is it the expression of an intention?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65r.2) <span class="fac">[65r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65r-2}

But could not a higher authority assure me that I do not know the truth? So that I would have to say “Teach me!” But then my eyes would have to be opened.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65r.3) <span class="fac">[65r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65r-3}

It is part of the language-game with personal names that everyone knows their name with the greatest certainty.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65r.4+65v.1) <span class="fac">[65r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65r.webp),[65v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65r-4--65v-1}

April 20, 1951

It could be that, whenever I say “I know it,” it turns out to be false. (Point out)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65v.2) <span class="fac">[65v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65v-2}

But I might still not be able to help myself, & would continue to assure “I know –”. But how did the human being learn this expression?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65v.3) <span class="fac">[65v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65v-3}

“I know it” can mean: It is already known to me – but also: It is certainly so.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#65v.4+66r.1) <span class="fac">[65v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/65v.webp),[66r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-65v-4--66r-1}

“I know that it is called ‘…’.” – How do you know that? – “I learned it.” Could I put “I know that, etc.” here instead of “It is called ‘…’”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#66r.2) <span class="fac">[66r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-66r-2}

Would it be possible to use the verb “to know” only in the question “How do you know that?” which follows a simple assertion? – Instead of “I already know that,” one says “That is known to me”; & this only follows the communication of the fact. But what does one say instead of “I know what that is”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#66r.3) <span class="fac">[66r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-66r-3}

But does “I know that that is a tree” say something different from “That is a tree”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#66r.4+66v.1) <span class="fac">[66r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66r.webp),[66v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-66r-4--66v-1}

Instead of “I know what that is,” one could say “I can say what that is.” And if one were to adopt this way of expressing oneself, what would become of “I know that that is…”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#66v.2) <span class="fac">[66v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/66v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-66v-2}

Back to the question of whether “I know that that is a…” says something different from “That is a…”. – The first sentence mentions a person, the second does not. But that does not show that they have different meanings. In any case, one often replaces the first form with the second & often gives the latter a special intonation. Because one speaks differently when making an undisputed statement, & when maintaining it against a contradiction.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#67r.1+67v.1) <span class="fac">[67r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/67r.webp),[67v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-67r-1--67v-1}

But doesn’t saying “I know that…” mean that I am in a certain state, whereas the mere assertion “That is a…” doesn’t say this? And yet, one often answers such an assertion with “How do you know that?” – “But only because the fact that I make this assertion shows that I believe I know it.” – One could express it like this: In a zoo, the sign could read “This is a zebra”; but not “I know that this is a zebra.” “I know” only has sense when a person utters it. _Then_, however, it is irrelevant whether the utterance is “I know…”, or “That is…”.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#67v.2) <span class="fac">[67v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-67v-2}

How does one learn to recognize the state of knowledge?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#67v.3) <span class="fac">[67v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/67v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-67v-3}

One could more readily speak of recognizing a state where it says “I know what that is.” One can convince oneself here that one actually possesses this knowledge.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#67v.4+68r.1) <span class="fac">[67v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/67v.webp),[68r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-67v-4--68r-1}

“I know what kind of tree that is. – It is a chestnut.” “I know what kind of tree that is. I know that it is a chestnut.” The first statement sounds more natural than the second. One will only say “I know” a second time if one wants to particularly emphasize the certainty; for example, in order to anticipate a contradiction. The first “I know” means approximately: I can say. In another case, however, one could begin with the statement “That is a…”, and then, in response to a contradiction, say: “I know what kind of tree that is” and thus emphasize the certainty.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#68r.2+68v.1) <span class="fac">[68r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68r.webp),[68v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-68r-2--68v-1}

“I can say what kind of… that is, and with certainty.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#68v.2) <span class="fac">[68v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-68v-2}

Even if one can replace “I know that it is so” with “It is so,” one cannot replace the negation of the one with the negation of the other. With “I do not know…”, a new element enters the language-games.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#68v.3) <span class="fac">[68v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-68v-3}

21.04.1951

“L. W.” is my name. And if anyone disputed it, I would immediately establish countless connections that would confirm it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#68v.4+69r.1) <span class="fac">[68v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/68v.webp),[69r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/69r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-68v-4--69r-1}

“But I can imagine a person who makes all these connections, none of which correspond to reality. Why shouldn’t I be in a similar situation?” If I imagine that person, I also imagine a reality, a world that surrounds him; and him, as he thinks (& speaks) against this world.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#69r.2+69v.1) <span class="fac">[69r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/69r.webp),[69v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/69v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-69r-2--69v-1}

If someone tells me that his name is N.N., then it makes sense for me to ask him “Could you be mistaken about that?” This is a legitimate question in the language-game. And there is a sense to the answers Yes and No. – Of course, even this answer is not infallible, i.e., it may turn out to be wrong, but that does not take away from the question “Can you be mistaken…?” and the answer “No” their sense.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#69v.2) <span class="fac">[69v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/69v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-69v-2}

The answer to the question “Can you be mistaken about that?” gives the statement a certain weight. The answer can also be: “I _believe_ not.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#69v.3+70r.1) <span class="fac">[69v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/69v.webp),[70r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/70r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-69v-3--70r-1}

But couldn’t one answer the question “Can you…?” by saying: “I will describe the case to you and you can then judge for yourself whether I can be mistaken”? For example, if it concerns the name of the person, the case could be that the person has never used this name, but remembers having read it on a document – and on the other hand, the answer could be: “I have used this name all my life, I have been called that by all people.” If _that_ is not equivalent to the answer “I cannot be mistaken about that,” then it has no sense at all. And quite clearly, this indicates a very important difference.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#70r.2+70v.1+71r.1) <span class="fac">[70r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/70r.webp),[70v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/70v.webp),[71r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/71r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-70r-2--70v-1--71r-1}

One could, for example, describe the certainty of the sentence that water boils at approximately 100˚ C. It is not, for example, a sentence that I once heard, like this or that one, which I could name. I performed the experiment myself in school. The sentence is a very elementary one in our textbooks, which can be trusted in such matters because… – One can now oppose all of this with examples that show that people have considered this and that to be certain, which later, in our opinion, has proven to be false. But this argument is worthless. To say that in the end, we can only cite such reasons that _we_ consider to be reasons, says nothing. Can it not also happen that one believes today that one has recognized an error of earlier times, and later realizes that the first view was correct? etc. I believe that there is a misunderstanding of the essence of our language-games at the bottom of this.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#71r.2) <span class="fac">[71r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/71r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-71r-2}

What reason do I have to trust the textbooks of experimental physics? I have no reason not to trust them. And I trust them. I know how such books come about – or rather, I believe I know. I have some evidence, but it is not very far-reaching and is of a very scattered nature. I have heard, seen, and read things.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#71v.1) <span class="fac">[71v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/71v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-71v-1}

22.04.1951

There is always the danger of wanting to recognize the meaning by considering the expression and the mood in which one uses it, instead of always thinking about the practice. That is why one repeats the expression to oneself so often, because it is as if one had to see in it and in the feeling that one has what one is looking for.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#71v.2) <span class="fac">[71v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/71v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-71v-2}

23.04.1951

Should I say, “I believe in physics,” or “I know that physics is true”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#71v.3+72r.1) <span class="fac">[71v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/71v.webp),[72r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/72r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-71v-3--72r-1}

I am taught that under _such_ circumstances, _this_ happens. It has been discovered by performing the experiment a couple of times. Of course, all of this would not prove anything if it were not the case that other experiences, which form a system with it, lie around this experience. So, not only drop tests have been carried out, but also tests on air resistance, and so on. In the end, however, I rely on these experiences, or on reports of them, and I regulate my own actions without any hesitation. – But has this trust not also proven itself? As far as I can judge, yes.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#72v.1) <span class="fac">[72v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/72v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-72v-1}

In a courtroom, the statement of a physicist that water boils at approximately 100˚ C would certainly be accepted as true. If I were to distrust this statement, what could I do to disprove it? Should I conduct experiments myself? What would they prove?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#72v.2) <span class="fac">[72v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/72v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-72v-2}

But what if the physicist's statement were superstition, and it is just as false that the verdict should be based on it, as that it should be based on an ordeal?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#72v.3+73r.1) <span class="fac">[72v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/72v.webp),[73r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-72v-3--73r-1}

That another person has, in my _opinion_, made a mistake, is no reason to assume that I am now mistaken. – But isn’t it a reason to assume that I _might_ be mistaken? There is no reason for any _uncertainty_ in my judgment or action.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#73r.2) <span class="fac">[73r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-73r-2}

The judge could say, “That is the truth – as far as a person can recognize it.” – But what would this addition achieve? (“beyond all reasonable doubt”)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#73r.3+73v.1) <span class="fac">[73r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73r.webp),[73v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-73r-3--73v-1}

Is it wrong that I base my actions on the sentence of physics? Should I say that I have no good reason for it? Is not that precisely what we call a ‘good reason’?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#73v.2) <span class="fac">[73v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-73v-2}

Suppose we met people who did not consider this to be a valid reason. Well, how do we imagine that? Instead, they consult an oracle. (And we consider them to be primitive because of it.) Is it wrong that they consult an oracle and base their actions on it? – If we call this “wrong,” are we not already starting from our language-game and _combating_ theirs?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#73v.3+74r.1) <span class="fac">[73v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/73v.webp),[74r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/74r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-73v-3--74r-1}

And are we right or wrong in combating it? Of course, one could support our approach with all sorts of slogans.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#74r.2) <span class="fac">[74r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/74r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-74r-2}

Where two principles, which cannot be reconciled with each other, really meet, each declares the other to be a fool and a heretic.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#74r.3) <span class="fac">[74r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/74r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-74r-3}

I said that I would ‘combat’ the other, – but would I not also give him _reasons_? Yes; but how far do these reasons go? In the end, the reasons lead to _persuasion_. (Think about what happens when missionaries convert the natives.)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#74v.1+75r.1) <span class="fac">[74v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/74v.webp),[75r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-74v-1--75r-1}

If I now say “I know that the water in the kettle on the gas flame will not freeze, but boil,” then I seem to be just as justified in this “I know” as in _any_ other. ‘If I know something, I know _that_.’ – Or do I know, with even _greater_ certainty, that the person opposite me is my old friend so-and-so? And how does that compare with the sentence that I look with two _eyes_, & they will see when I look in the mirror? – I do not know for certain what I should answer to that. – But there is indeed a difference between the cases. If the water on the flame freezes, I will certainly be astonished to the highest degree, but I will assume an influence that is still unknown to me & perhaps leave the matter to physicists to judge. – But what could make me doubt that this person here is N.N., whom I have known for years? Here, a doubt seems to bring everything else with it & create chaos.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#75r.2+75v.1) <span class="fac">[75r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75r.webp),[75v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-75r-2--75v-1}

That is to say: If I were contradicted from all sides: that he is not as I always knew (and I use “knew” intentionally), then in this case the basis of all judgment would be taken away from me.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#75v.2) <span class="fac">[75v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-75v-2}

Does that mean: “I can only judge because things behave in such and such a way (as if good-naturedly)”?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#75v.3) <span class="fac">[75v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-75v-3}

But would it really be _unthinkable_ that I would remain in the saddle, even if the facts were to buck so much?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#75v.4+76r.1) <span class="fac">[75v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/75v.webp),[76r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/76r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-75v-4--76r-1}

I would be placed in a situation by certain events in which I could no longer continue the old game. In which I would be torn out of the _certainty_ of the game. Yes, doesn’t it seem obvious that a language-game is only possible through certain facts?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#76r.2) <span class="fac">[76r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/76r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-76r-2}

It would then seem as if the language-game, the facts that make it possible, must ‘_show_’. (But that is not the case.) Can one say that only a certain regularity in the events makes induction possible? Of course, ‘possible’ must be ‘_logically possible_’.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#76r.3+76v.1) <span class="fac">[76r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/76r.webp),[76v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/76v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-76r-3--76v-1}

Should I say: Even if an irregularity were to suddenly occur in the course of nature, that _should_ not throw me off balance. I could, as before, draw conclusions – but whether one would call that “induction” is another question.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#76v.2) <span class="fac">[76v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/76v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-76v-2}

Under certain circumstances, one says “You can rely on it”; & this assurance can be justified, or unjustified, in everyday language, & it can also be justified even if what was predicted did not come to pass. _There is a language-game_ in which the assurance is used.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#77r.1) <span class="fac">[77r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/77r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-77r-1}

April 24, 1951

If anatomy were being discussed, I would say: “I know that 12 pairs of nerves originate from the brain.” I have never seen these nerves, and even an expert has only observed them in a few specimens. – But here the word “I know” is used correctly.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#77r.2+77v.1) <span class="fac">[77r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/77r.webp),[77v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/77v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-77r-2--77v-1}

Now, it is also true that “I know” can be used in the connections that Moore mentions, at least _under certain circumstances_. (However, I do not know what “I know that I am a human being” means. But one could also give it a sense.) I can imagine circumstances for each of these sentences that would make it a move in one of our language-games, thereby causing it to lose all its philosophically surprising quality.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#77v.2) <span class="fac">[77v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/77v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-77v-2}

The strange thing is that in such a case I always want to say (although it is false): “I know that – as far as one can know it.” That is incorrect, but there is something correct behind it.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#77v.3+78r.1) <span class="fac">[77v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/77v.webp),[78r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/78r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-77v-3--78r-1}

“Can you be mistaken in that this color is called ‘green’ in German?” My answer to that can only be “No.” If I said, “Yes, – because a delusion is always possible,” that would mean nothing. Is the added remark something unknown to the other? And how is it known to me?


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#78r.2) <span class="fac">[78r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/78r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-78r-2}

Does that mean, however, that it would be unthinkable that the word “green” here springs from a kind of promise or momentary confusion? Don’t we know such cases? – One can also say to someone: “You may not have made a promise –”. That is about: “Think about it again!” – But these precautions only make sense if they come to an end at some point. A doubt without end is not even a doubt.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#78v.1) <span class="fac">[78v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/78v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-78v-1}

It also means nothing to say: “The German name for this color is _certainly_ ‘green’ – unless I am making a mistake right now or am somehow confused.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#78v.2) <span class="fac">[78v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/78v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-78v-2}

Shouldn't one insert this clause into _all_ language-games? (Which would reveal their nonsensical nature.)


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#78v.3+79r.1) <span class="fac">[78v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/78v.webp),[79r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-78v-3--79r-1}

If one says: “Certain sentences must be excluded from doubt,” then it seems as though I should include these sentences, e.g., that I am called L.W., in a book about logic. For if it belongs to the description of the language-game, then it belongs to logic. But the fact that I am called L.W. does not belong to such a description. The language-game that operates with personal names can certainly exist even if I am mistaken about my name – but it presupposes that it is nonsensical to say that the majority of people are mistaken about their names.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#79r.2+79v.1) <span class="fac">[79r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79r.webp),[79v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-79r-2--79v-1}

On the other hand, it is correct if I assert “I cannot be mistaken about my name,” and incorrect if I say “Perhaps I am mistaken.” But that does not mean that it is nonsensical for others to doubt what I declare to be certain.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#79v.2) <span class="fac">[79v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-79v-2}

Not being able to be mistaken in one’s native language about the designation of certain things is simply the usual case.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#79v.3) <span class="fac">[79v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-79v-3}

“I cannot be mistaken about this” is simply a kind of assertion.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#79v.4+80r.1) <span class="fac">[79v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/79v.webp),[80r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-79v-4--80r-1}

Certain and uncertain memory. If certain memory were not, in general, more reliable, i.e., if it were not confirmed by other verifications more than uncertain memory, then the expression of certainty and uncertainty would not have its present function in language.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#80r.2) <span class="fac">[80r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80r.webp)</span> {#ms-176-80r-2}

“I cannot be mistaken about this” – but how, if I then am mistaken? Isn’t that possible? But does that make the expression “I cannot be mistaken, etc.” nonsensical? Or would it be better to say instead “I can hardly be mistaken about this”? No, because this means something else.


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#80r.3+80v.1) <span class="fac">[80r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80r.webp),[80v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-80r-3--80v-1}

“I cannot be mistaken about this; and in the worst case, I turn my sentence into a norm.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#80v.2) <span class="fac">[80v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-80v-2}

“I cannot be mistaken about this: I was with him today.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#80v.3) <span class="fac">[80v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-80v-3}

“I cannot be mistaken about this; but if something should seem to speak against my sentence, I will, against appearances, stick to it.”


### [Ms-176](/ms-176/#80v.4) <span class="fac">[80v\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-176/80v.webp)</span> {#ms-176-80v-4}

“I cannot be mistaken, etc.” assigns my assertion its place in the game. But it refers essentially to _me_, not to the game in general.

If I am mistaken in my assertion, that does not deprive the language-game of its use.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#1r.2) <span class="fac">[1r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-1r-2}

April 25, 1951

“I cannot be mistaken in this” is a common sentence used to indicate the degree of certainty of a statement. And it is justified only in its everyday use.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#1r.3) <span class="fac">[1r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-1r-3}

But what on earth is it good for if I can – admittedly – be mistaken in it, and thus also in the sentence that it is supposed to support?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#1r.4) <span class="fac">[1r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-1r-4}

Or should I say, the sentence excludes a certain _kind_ of error.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#1r.5+1v.1) <span class="fac">[1r\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1r.webp),[1v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-1r-5--1v-1}

“He told me that today; I cannot be mistaken in this.” – But what if it turns out to be false?! – Shouldn’t one make a distinction in the way something ‘turns out to be false’? – How can it be _proven_ that my statement was false? Here, evidence is set against evidence, and it must be _decided_ which one should yield.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#1v.2) <span class="fac">[1v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/1v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-1v-2}

But if one raises the objection: What if I suddenly, as it were, wake up and say, “Now I imagined that I am called L.W.” – who says that I will not wake up again and then explain _this_ as a strange delusion, and so on?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2r.1) <span class="fac">[2r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2r-1}

One can, of course, imagine a case, and there are cases in which, after ‘waking up,’ one never doubts again what was an illusion and what was reality. But such a case, or its possibility, does not discredit the sentence “I cannot be mistaken in this.”


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2r.2) <span class="fac">[2r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2r-2}

Otherwise, wouldn’t it discredit all assertions?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2r.3) <span class="fac">[2r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2r-3}

I cannot be mistaken in this, – but I may, rightly or wrongly, decide that I was not in a fit state to judge.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2v.1) <span class="fac">[2v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2v-1}

If this were to happen always or often, it would indeed completely change the character of the language-game.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2v.2) <span class="fac">[2v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2v-2}

There is a difference between an error for which, as it were, a place is reserved in the game, and a complete violation of the rules that occurs exceptionally.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#2v.3+3r.1+3v.1) <span class="fac">[2v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/2v.webp),[3r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/3r.webp),[3v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/3v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-2v-3--3r-1--3v-1}

I can also convince others that I cannot be ‘mistaken about it’. I say to someone: “So & so was here this _morning_ & told me this & that.” If it is surprising, he might ask me: “You cannot be mistaken about that?” This might mean: “Is it also certain that it happened this _morning_?” or rather: “Did you also understand him correctly?” – It is easy to see how, through certain explanations, I could show that I was not mistaken about the time, & also, that I did not misunderstand the story. But all of this cannot show that I did not dream the whole thing, or that I imagined it in a dreamlike way. It can also not show that I did not perhaps slip up _throughout_. (Such a thing does happen.)


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#3v.2) <span class="fac">[3v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/3v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-3v-2}

(I once told someone – in English – that the shape of a certain branch was characteristic of the branch of an elm, which the other person disputed. We then came across ashes, and I said, “You see, here are the branches I was talking about.” To which he replied: “But that’s an ash” – and I: “I always meant ash when I said elm.”)


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#3v.3+4r.1) <span class="fac">[3v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/3v.webp),[4r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/4r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-3v-3--4r-1}

That is, the possibility of an _error_ can be eliminated in certain (and frequent) cases. – This is also how calculation errors are eliminated. For if a calculation has been checked many times, one can no longer say: “Its correctness is only _very probable_ – because an error may still have crept in.” For suppose it now seems that an error has been discovered – why should we not suspect an error _here_?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#4r.2+4v.1) <span class="fac">[4r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/4r.webp),[4v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/4v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-4r-2--4v-1}

I cannot be mistaken in that 12 × 12 = 144. And one cannot set the _mathematical_ certainty against the relative uncertainty of sentences of experience. For the mathematical sentence was obtained through a series of actions that do not differ in any way from actions in the rest of life & which are equally subject to forgetting, oversight, and deception.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#4v.2) <span class="fac">[4v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/4v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-4v-2}

Can I now prophesy that people will never overturn today’s calculations, never say that now they know how it really is? But would that justify a doubt on our part?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#5r.1) <span class="fac">[5r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/5r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-5r-1}

If the sentence 12 × 12 = 144 is exempt from doubt, then non-mathematical sentences must also be.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#5r.2+5v.1) <span class="fac">[5r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/5r.webp),[5v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/5v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-5r-2--5v-1}

26.04.1951

But one can raise several objections to this. – Firstly, “12 × 12 etc.” is indeed a _mathematical_ sentence, and one can infer from this that only such sentences are in a position to be so. And if this inference is not justified, then there should be an equally certain sentence that deals with the process of calculation but is not mathematical. – I am thinking of a sentence of this kind: “The calculation ‘12 × 12’, when performed by arithmeticians, will, in the vast majority of cases, yield ‘144’.” No one would dispute this sentence; no one would dispute it, and it is, of course, not a mathematical sentence. But does it have the certainty of the mathematical?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#5v.2) <span class="fac">[5v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/5v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-5v-2}

The mathematical sentence, as it were, has officially had the stamp of indisputability affixed to it. That is: “Argue about other things; _this_ is certain, it is an anchor around which your dispute can revolve.”


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#5v.3) <span class="fac">[5v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/5v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-5v-3}

And one cannot say of a _sentence_ that _I_ am called L.W. Nor of the sentence that the such-&-such people calculated the such-&-such calculation correctly.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#6r.1) <span class="fac">[6r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/6r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-6r-1}

One could say that the sentences of mathematics are petrified. – The sentence “I am called…” is not. But those who, like me, have the overwhelming evidence also regard it as _incontrovertible_. And not out of thoughtlessness. For the fact that the evidence is overwhelming consists precisely in the fact that we do not _have_ to yield to any opposing evidence. We therefore have here a safeguard similar to the one that makes the sentences of mathematics incontrovertible.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#6r.2+6v.1) <span class="fac">[6r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/6r.webp),[6v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/6v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-6r-2--6v-1}

The question ‘But could you not now be in a delusion, and perhaps later find out that you were?’ could also be raised for each sentence of the multiplication table.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#6v.2) <span class="fac">[6v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/6v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-6v-2}

“I cannot be mistaken that I have just had lunch.” Yes, if I say to someone, “I have just had lunch,” he may believe that I am lying, or not in my right mind, but he will not believe that I am _mistaken_. Yes, the assumption that I could be mistaken makes no sense here.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#6v.3+7r.1) <span class="fac">[6v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/6v.webp),[7r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-6v-3--7r-1}

But that is not true. I could, for example, immediately after lunch, without knowing it, doze off and sleep for an hour, and then believe that I have just eaten. But I am at least distinguishing between different kinds of error here.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#7r.2) <span class="fac">[7r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-7r-2}

I could ask: “_How_ could I be mistaken that I am called L.W.?” And I can say: I do not see how it would be possible.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#7r.3) <span class="fac">[7r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-7r-3}

How could I be mistaken in the assumption that I have never been to the moon?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#7r.4+7v.1) <span class="fac">[7r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7r.webp),[7v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-7r-4--7v-1}

If I said, “I have not been to the moon – but I could be mistaken,” that would be absurd. For even the thought that I could, by unknown means, have been transported there in my sleep, _would not give me the right_ to speak of a possible mistake here. I am playing the game _wrongly_ if I do so.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#7v.2) <span class="fac">[7v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-7v-2}

I have a right to say “I cannot be mistaken here,” even if I am mistaken.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#7v.3+8r.1) <span class="fac">[7v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/7v.webp),[8r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-7v-3--8r-1}

There is a difference between learning in school what is right and wrong in mathematics, and my explaining myself that I cannot be mistaken in a sentence.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#8r.2) <span class="fac">[8r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-8r-2}

I am adding something special to what is generally established.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#8r.3) <span class="fac">[8r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-8r-3}

But what about, for example, anatomy (or a large part of it)? Is not what it describes also beyond all doubt?


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#8r.4) <span class="fac">[8r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-8r-4}

Even if I came to a people who believe that people are transported to the moon in their dreams, I could not say to them: “I have never been to the moon. – Of course, I could be mistaken.” And to their question “Can you not be mistaken?” I would have to answer: No.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#8v.1) <span class="fac">[8v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-8v-1}

What practical consequences does it have if I make a statement and add that I cannot be mistaken in it? (Instead, I could also add: “I can be mistaken in this no more than in the fact that I am called L.W.”) The other person may still doubt my statement. But not only will he, if he trusts me, allow himself to be instructed by me, but he will also draw certain conclusions about my behavior from my conviction.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#8v.2+9r.1) <span class="fac">[8v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/8v.webp),[9r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-8v-2--9r-1}

The sentence “I cannot be mistaken in this” will certainly be used in practice. One might, however, doubt whether it is to be understood in a strictly logical sense, or whether it is rather an exaggeration, perhaps used only for the purpose of persuasion. 27.04.1951


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#9r.2) <span class="fac">[9r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/9r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-9r-2}

One could speak of fundamental principles of human research.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#9r.3+9v.1+10r.1) <span class="fac">[9r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/9r.webp),[9v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/9v.webp),[10r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-9r-3--9v-1--10r-1}

I fly from here to a continent where people have only vague, or even no, knowledge of the possibility of flying. I tell them that I have just flown from … to them. They ask me if I could be mistaken. – They apparently have a false image of how things are. (If I were packed into a box, it would be possible for me to be mistaken about the method of transport. If I simply tell them that I cannot be mistaken, this might not convince them; but if I describe the process to them, they will certainly not question the possibility of an _error_. However, they might – even if they trust me – believe that I dreamed it, or that some _magic_ made me imagine it.)


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#10r.2) <span class="fac">[10r\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-10r-2}

‘If I don’t trust _the_ evidence, why should I trust any evidence at all?’


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#10r.3) <span class="fac">[10r\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-10r-3}

Is it not difficult to distinguish between the cases in which I am _not_ – & those in which I can _hardly_ be mistaken? Is it always clear to which type a case belongs? I don't think so.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#10r.4+10v.1) <span class="fac">[10r\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10r.webp),[10v\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-10r-4--10v-1}

Now, however, there are certain typical cases in which I can rightly say that I cannot be mistaken, and Moore has given some examples of such cases. I can enumerate various typical cases, but I cannot give a general characteristic. (N.N. cannot be mistaken in that he flew from America to England a few days ago. Only if he is foolish can he consider anything else possible.)


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#10v.2) <span class="fac">[10v\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10v.webp)</span> {#ms-177-10v-2}

If someone believes that he flew from America to England a few days ago, then I believe that he cannot be _mistaken_ in this. Likewise, if someone says that he is now sitting at the table and writing.


### [Ms-177](/ms-177/#10v.3+11r.1) <span class="fac">[10v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/10v.webp),[11r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-177/11r.webp)</span> {#ms-177-10v-3--11r-1}

“But if I cannot be mistaken even in these cases, is it not possible that I am in a state of anesthesia?” If I am in a state of anesthesia and if the anesthesia deprives me of consciousness, then I am not really talking and thinking now. I cannot seriously assume that I am dreaming now. Whoever says “I am dreaming” while dreaming, even if he is talking audibly, is as wrong as if he says “It is raining” in his dream, while it is actually raining. Even if his dream is actually related to the sound of rain.
